Notice · page 19

Membership statistics as at 30th June 1994

Total membership 668

During the twelve months ending 30th June 1994 we accepted 123 applications for membership but during the same period lost 141 who had resigned, been deleted, etc. - a net loss for the year of 18.

New annual subscription rates were agreed at the Annual General Meeting. As from 1st July 1995 the rates will be:

£25 ... 20 years of age or over on 1st July £15 ... 17 years of age but not yet 20 on 1st July £15 ... Associate members and second-claim members £10 ... under 17 on 1st July Current figures are £20, £10 and £6

Special rates exist for Family membership and Husband/Wife or Partners. Contact Carl Lawton [phone number withheld] for details.

This must be regarded as one of John's finest ever runs, with no wind or altitude assistance. The race was won by Michael Johnson in 19.94 with Fredericks 3rd - the second time he had been beaten by Regis in a week. What a brilliant night for fast Belgrave times, for Du'aine Ladejo made a third improvement on his best one lap time while equalling Black's fastest British time for the year and levelling the score to two all as his rival came home 4th. 200m: (0.0) 2, J.Regis 20.01. 400m: 3, D.Ladejo 44.94. 17 Zurich, Switzerland., IAAF Grand Prix. 5000m: 13, G.Staines 13:45.57. 24 Goteborg, IAAF Permit Meet. 2 miles: 6, G.Staines 8:29.12. September 3 Paris, IAAF Grand Prix Final. As with others who had contested the European and Commonwealth Games, jet lag began to catch up on Ladejo. Americans, Roger Black and Kenyan Kitur who had all stayed in Europe got the better of our man. 400m: 7, D.Ladejo 45.80. 4 Sheffield, McDonald Games. 150m: (+0.9) 2, J.Regis 15.15 (behind Christie's 14.97 National Record). 300m: 3, D.Ladejo 32.73. JT: 12, N.Bevan 64.82. 6 Madrid, IAAF Permit Meet. 200m: 2, J.Regis 20.35. 13 Poona, India, IAAF Permit Meet. 5000m: 4, G.Staines 13:56.6. 15 Tokyo, Toto International. 200m: 3, J.Regis 20.55.

Report · page 20

Shirley confirms form — Europa Cup Combined Events, Bressanone, Italy, 2-3 Jul.

EUROPA CUP COMBINED EVENTS BRESSANONE, ITALY, 2-3 JUL.

Shirley confirms form

Simon Shirley entered his second decathlon in Europe for the year when he represented GB in the Europa Cup for Combined Events in stifling heat at Bressanone, Italy. The GB team put together a solid performance finishing 2nd, ahead of Russia. Simon succumbed to the heat on the second day having ended day one with 4,230 points but held on well to finish 4th overall just six points light of his Club Record with 7938 points. The team performance gained GB promotion to next year's "Super League" Division of the Europa Cup. The "Super League" Cup was held the same weekend in Lyon, France, and it is worth noting that the GB team performance would have placed them a respectable 5th in that competition. 1, V.Kolpakov (UKR) 8257; 2, L.Lobodin (UKR) 8176; 3, A.Kruger (GBR) 7963; 4, S.Shirley (GBR) 7938 (10.94, 7.52, 13.75, 2.04, 48.83/14.99, 38.04, 4.60, 57.54, 4:31.47). Teams: 1, Ukraine 24,204; 2, Great Britain 23,464; 3, Russia 23,136.

Report · page 20

Chaston looks the part — European Cup, Birmingham, 25-26 Jul

EUROPEAN CUP BIRMINGHAM, 25-26 JUL

Chaston looks the part

Justin Chaston is really beginning to look the part as an international steeplechaser. His sub-8:30 run had him dicing with Steffan Brand of Germany but it must be said that the Italian Alessandro Lambruschini was in a class apart from the rest. Other Belgravians were less than ecstatic about their performances, however. John Regis was only operating at 95% effort in the sprint relay after pulling out of the 200m with an achilles niggle; a worrying sign with the major championships just around the corner. Not at all happy either with his day's work was Paul Edwards who only allowed two of his efforts to be measured and Gary Staines' confidence also took a knock in the 10k as he barely cracked the half-hour. In the vault Mike Edwards sailed over his early heights of 5.00 and 5.20m but once again 5.40 the sort of height needed to get into big 'Games' finals, proved to be just out of reach. (a) - 25th, (b) -26th 10000m (a): 5, G.Staines 29:57.27. 3000mSC (b): 3, J.Chaston 8:29.99. PV (b): =5, M.Edwards 5.20. SP (b) 7, P.Edwards 18.10.

Report · page 21

Euro gold for Ladejo — Helsinki, Finland, 7-14 Aug

Helsinki: largest city, capital and chief seaport of Finland: but as far as the athletic cognoscenti are concerned, home of the 1952 Olympics, scene of so many glorious athletic feats over the years and the setting for the 1994 European Games. Eight Belgravians earned the right to represent their country but Decathlete Simon Shirley turned down the opportunity so that he could concentrate on the Commonwealth competition while we were kept guessing about John Regis' intentions, troubled as he was by injury problems.

10,000 metres

The opening day of competition saw a much better race from Gary Staines than he had put together recently. Just bear in mind that four years ago this man was silver medallist in Split, Yugolslavia, over 5,000m but has since then been travelling a hard road littered with set-backs. The horrendous scar left by an operation on one of his achilles tendons makes one realise just how much determination there is in an athlete who competes at this level. Here he was again at Europe's premier athletic event, battling with the best that the 'old world' could offer.

After an opening circuit of 72 secs. the Belgian Rousseau threw in a sub-60 and a "gutty" race was truly under way. The pack was tightly knit as Franke of Germany took on most of the leading, none of the main players falling away. At 7k Gary was right in the thick of it and felt that he had as much chance of a medal strike as anyone else, but a kilometre later a 62.5 lap split the field and from there on it was a case of fighting for survival. Gary did well to come up with his season's best. 1, A.Anton (Spain) 28:06.03; 2, V. Rousseau (Belgium) 28:06.63; 3, S.Franke (Germany) 28:07.95; ... 15, G.Staines 28:25.60.

200 metres

It was a disappointment to learn that our Champion John Regis would not be taking part due to his achilles tendon problems, and rumours that he may later run just in the sprint relay also proved to be unfounded. The way was left clear therefore for Geir Moen who won the final in 20.30, part of a startling upsurge in Norwegian athletics. Phil Goedluck, having the season of his life, went successfully through the heats with a 3rd place 21.01 and then a second round 4th in 20.99 before coming to the end of the road with 7th and 21.11 in his semi-final.

400 metres

Cards were kept pretty close to the chest during the opening round. Defending Champion Roger Black was clear in his heat with 45.88 while in the next race on the track our own Du'aine Ladejo ran a throttled back 46.50 to shade the Italian Aimar. Next day a similar game was played out. This time Ladejo ran first with a cruising 45.70 and when Black took semi-final 2 just 9/100ths slower we knew that a superb final was in prospect with the two Britons head and shoulders above the rest.

And so to act three of the drama: the day of the final. Black was drawn two lanes inside the Belgravian and he went out hard, fully believing in himself as a Champion should and coming out of the final bend just ahead of the field. But nowadays Ladejo believes he can handle any type of situation and so it proved. He had the strength to run down the leader over the last 50m and crossed the line with arms outstretched almost as wide as his smile. Britain 1st and 2nd in a classic confrontation and a 'gold' for Belgrave. 1, D.Ladejo 45.09; 2, R.Black 45.20; 3, M.Rusterholz (Switz) 45.96.

3000 metres Steeplechase

John Bicourt's record lives on. With Justin Chaston now in the sort of territory where he is capable of living with a fast pace it was a possibility that he might get dragged out here to a low 8:20s clocking. With the first four and four fastest to progress from the semi's, Justin's 7th and 8:31.08 in the fastest of the preliminaries earned him a run in the next day's final where Italy's Lambruschini was the outstanding favourite. Nothing is ever cut and dried in athletics, however. The Italian had the misfortune to fall after only one and a half laps. Compatriot Panetta helped him to his feet and they gradually eased back into a pack that had lost momentum without them. Lambruschini still had enough in hand to see him to the 'gold' but Chaston suffered during the severe acceleration towards the end. He was 3rd Briton behind a resurgent Mark Rowland (4th) 'and Tom Hanlon (10th). 1, A.Lambruschini (Italy) 8:22.40; 2, A.Carosi (Italy) 8:23.53; 3, W.van Dijk (Belgium) 8:24.86; ... 11, J.Chaston 8:36.83.

Pole Vault

A vault of 5.65m was needed to guarantee a place in the final, 13cm higher than Mike Edwards has ever done and 35cm better than his '94 best. Opening up at 5.20m, our man needed all three attempts to lever himself over the bar with the subsequent 5.40m proving just out of reach. Mike had to be satisfied with =12th in his pool. Gataullin of Russia eventually took gold at 6.00m with no fewer than six former Soviets among the final twelve.

Shot Putt

This is another event where qualification has been made tougher by the break-up of the Soviet Union - but overall the standard was not that high. Paul Edwards missed out on a berth in the final by just 10cm as he hit 18.54m. Ukrainian Aleksandr Klimenko won at 20.78m with two others from his country making it a clean sweep for the Eastern European plainsmen.

4x400 metres Relay

Usually one of the most competitive of events, the 4x400m relay did not provide as much as excitement as normal with only a straight final being required in spite of the fact that a record 48 nations were taking part in Games. With individual gold and silver medals already in the bag the British should have been invincible, and so they were. The race was set up nicely by McKenzie and Whittle, and then Black stormed away from the field with a 43.96 stint to leave Du'aine Ladejo the honour of a lonely last lap of 44.19 for his second 'gold'. To decimate the field even further, France and Russia were then disqualified, leaving a massive 4 second gap to the next team home - Italy. Later they were reinstated giving: 1, G.Britain 2:59:13; 2, France 3:01.11; 3, Russia 3:03.10.

Report · page 22

Five medals for Bels in Victoria — 22-28 August, Victoria, Canada, 1994 Commonwealth Games.

The Commonwealth Games might be classed as friendly, but once the gun fires they are as competitive as any and as far as the track events go the standard is only lower than the Olympic Games and the World Championships. Our top two men both found themselves scalped by Africans - but what about that medals table - Belgrave ranked 5th among the nations and with just a little more luck that medal haul could have been even higher.

Of course we were greatly saddened to hear news of Paul Edward's positive drugs test. But while Paul was raised from his sickbed where he had been suffering from a stomach complaint and made his way home, we followed the progress of the remaining nine Belgravians.

200 metres

John Regis had been through several weeks without competition. He had missed the "Europeans" with an achilles tendon niggle, the most recent in a series of problems. Nevertheless, he was the fastest man in the World for 1994 with that scintillating 19.87 British Record at Sestrierre. Would his injuries hold up; would he be sharp enough to withstand the African onslaught?

Round 1 had him cruising to an easy 21.01 win in the 8th of 9 heats and a few hours later it was time to test the Regis body a little more - the result a 20.36 Games Record. He wasn't the only one to impress though, for Nigeria's Oluyemi posted a 20.39 and Fredericks a 20.40.

Come the semis after a rest day and the two main contenders were rightly separated, both keeping a little in store for the final but both winning. And so they went to their blocks for the showdown. World Champion Fredericks drawn just inside the Belgravian and shooting him a final glance before going through the ritual of coiling up into the starting position.

Normally, at the starting signal, Regis makes great inroads into the field, and so he did again here in spite of straining an adductor only 50 metres out. But the Namibian was the one man who held him perfectly and entering the straight, now they were into Frederick's favourite territory. He floated to the lead and once there gave an awesome display of high knee lift to stretch away to the title. (+1.5) 1, F.Fredericks (Namibia) 19.97; 2, J.Regis (England) 20.25; 3, D.Effiong (Nigeria) 20.40.

400 metres

Another four rounds of major championship 400m running in two days may just have stretched Du'aine Ladejo too far after Helsinki. He looked so comfortable with 47.90, 46.26 and 45.23 as he progressed to the final but a certain cool customer from Kenya had yet to show his hand. Charles Gitonga was not worth a mention in the T&F Bible "Athletics 94" but we should all know that any Kenyan in a final is a threat. Du'aine drew lane six but could make no impression on the African out on the perimeter of the track. At 300m they were level but from there on the the race belonged to Gitonga.

A defeat did not seem to do our man any harm. He was obviously disappointed but his demeanour in accepting his fate won him many more admirers. 1, C.Gitonga (Kenya) 45.00; 2, D.Ladejo (England) 45.11; 3, S.Bada (Nigeria) 45.45.

Long Jump

Fred Salle's pb of 7.97 dated back to 1986, the year when he last made an appearance in this competition, winning silver in Edinburgh. But the 1994 Freddie had been looking sharp all summer and he pulled out a wind legal 7.85 jump in Group A that placed him 4th of the 12 qualifiers and updated his Club Record.

The Nigerian Eregbu had flown to a record 8.22 for his qualifying leap and his very first effort in the final, while not huge, proved to be more than anyone else would reach. Fred opened well with 7.87 for =2nd place with Culbert but then slipped to 3rd in round 2 when the Australian got in a back-up jump while Fred nj'd. As the competition wound on a bronze seemed more and more hopeful, especially when the Belgravian improved to 7.88 in round 5 but last ditch efforts from Ayodele of Nigeria and James of Canada, using a 2.7 tailwind to great advantage, turned bronze into 5th. Bad luck Fred but three improvements in the Club Record in one competition can't be bad. 1, O.Eregbu (Nigeria) 8.05; 2, D.Culbert (Australia) 8.00; 3, I.James (Canada) 7.93; ... 5, F.Salle 7.88 (+1.9) ( 7.87, x, 7.67, 7.67, 7.88, 7.63).

Triple Jump

Wearing the blue and white of Scotland John Mackenzie produced his longest ever outdoor leap of 16.12 to place 14th of the 12 qualifiers. As Scottish indoor record holder he has an indoor mark of 16.13 to his name and although he must have been disappointed at not reaching the final, particularly as 7th place went as cheaply as 16.02, you can't ask for more than a pb.

Pole Vault

Mike Edwards opened at 5.20 and went clear at his first attempt to share the lead but a handful of men were also successful at subsequent attempts and ominously the outstanding favourite from South Africa, Okert Brits, and the Aussie Miller had yet to come in. The tactics now began in earnest as Winter sat the next round out, Brits still waited, and the rest attempted 5.30. An early clearance here and Mike could start thinking seriously about a medal - but it wasn't to be. Three men went clear at their first or second shots while for Mike it was three goes and out.

Welshman Winter surprised at 5.40 with another first time effort, Miller failed at 5.45, and incredibly Brits, with a pb of 5.85, found his opening height of 5.50 beyond him. Dramatic stuff. 1, N.Winter (Wales) 5.40; 2, C.Heywood (Canada) 5.30; 3, J.Miller (Australia) 5.30; ... 5, M.Edwards (England) 5.20 (5.20o, 5.30xxx).

Discus

This was always going to be a difficult event for an outsider to break into. Reiterer and Olukoju both launched 60 metre throws in round one and with Birchfield and England's Bob Weir putting the platter over the same line in round three, no one else came near. Darrin Morris held 6th place after the first time through the card, and then made the cut for three more throws by holding on to 8th after three rounds, but although peppering the field at 53-54 metres was unable to find the extra metre that would move him up any further. 1, W.Reiterer (Australia) 62.76; 2, A.Olukoju (Nigeria) 62.46; 3, R.Weir (England) 60.86; ... 8, D.Morris (Scotland) 54.98 (53.08, x, 54.98, 53.38, 54.42, 52.96).

Javelin

The bare facts say that Nigel Bevan was 4th from the end of round 1 and was still there at the end - but it was much more exciting than that. Hill's opening 81.84 stood up as best until Backley put one out to 82.74 in the third with the Kiwi Lovegrove also unleashing his best at this stage. The big Belgrave man was giving it everything but he couldn't quite hit it right and while fouling in his efforts slipped to 5th.

Round four and at last he got everything in line. The spear clearly landed over the 80 metre line; it didn't look gold but could well have been a medal winning distance. The few seconds wait until the electronic display flashed up the distance agonisingly ticked away and then, frustratingly, the news was spelled out - 80.38 - just 4cm from bronze. Nobody improved in the last two rounds and just as in the Auckland Games four years earlier Nigel was in that unluckiest position of all, 4th, with the first four positions all identical to 1990. 1, S.Backley (England) 82.74; 2, M.Hill (England) 81.84; 3, G.Lovegrove (New Zealand) 80.42; 4, N.Bevan (Wales) 80.38 (75.32, 76.38, x, x, 80.38, x).

Decathlon

For the TV comentators it was all Alex Kruger (England) and Jamie Quarry (Scotland) as far as the home countries were concerned. It took three-quarters of the competition before they woke up to the fact that the top Briton was going to be neither of these and that not only was he not an Australian turned Brit but was born in Nottingham. When you think of it, although Australian record holder, Belgravian Simon Shirley is as English as anyone in the team.

Canada's Mike Smith was always in the driving seat but it was very tight for the minor places. The two Australians Winter and Smith looked the most likely 2nd and 3rd placers and Simon was struggling with an ankle injury that was giving him great pain. With a couple of events to go he was within an ace of pulling out but the team physio worked a little magic, he hung on in there, and a super 63.80 javelin throw carried him to within one point of third place.

Now all you distance runners might well think the 1,500 metre race a doddle, but when it is the last of ten events and you weigh in at 91kg and measure 1.90m in your socks (that's 14½ stone and 6ft 3ins for you oldies!) it aint no joke. Before you read on though, take a look at the 1,500m rankings towards the back of this magazine and then remember that this man runs 10.89 for the 100 as well.

There was no doubt now that it was all or bust for Simon. Gradually working into the race, by the time he entered the back straight for the last time he was flying. It was an heroic run as he went away from the pack, clinched the bronze medal, set new club figures and came so close to the 8,000 barrier. 1, M.Smith (Canada) 8326; 2, P.Winter (Australia) 8074; 3, S.Shirley (England) 7980 (10.89 885, 7.11 840, 14.16 738, 2.04 840, 48.90 866, 14.87 865, 38.38 632, 4.60 790, 63.80 795, 4:32.4 729).

4x100m

With none of its recognised stars in the line-up the England team looked a bit makeshift but a 39.51 in the semi-final gave them a 2nd place to Canada that raised hopes a little. Our own 200 metre specialist Phil Goedluck was entrusted with the second bend and with Surrey 100 metre champion Terry Williams showing the form of his life they went even faster in the final for a completely unexpected set of bronze medals. One felt so pleased for Phil, on the verge of top class competition for years but still keeping up the day job (and night job too, no doubt) on Battersea Firestation's Blue Watch. 1, Canada 38.39; 2, Australia 38.88; 3, England (J.John, T.Box, P.Goedluck, T.Williams) 39.39.

4x400metres

The long relay never lacks drama, whether at club level or in the big championships. With a single circuit remaining Jamaica, with Garth Robinson carrying the baton, had a huge lead on the bunch - possibly more than 15 metres. Du'aine Ladejo had been rested up in the first round but then so had Robinson. It must be all over.

Into the home stretch, however, and Ladejo was eating up the ground. A couple of metres away from the line and the impossible was achieved. England took the last gold of the Games with a meeting record and the Belgravian's gold and individual leg of 44.1 must have gone a long way towards making that defeat in the individual event earlier in the week just a small blip on the road to glory. 1, England (D.McKenzie, P.Crampton, H.Patrick, D.Ladejo) 3:02.14. Ladejo contributed a 44.1 leg.

Report · page 23

Super Salle sets World Cup Alight — Crystal Palace, 9-11 Sep., IAAF World Cup

As Belgravians, we knew that Long Jumper Fred Salle was having a good summer but overnight he became a household name as he set the World Cup final alight as far as the British men's team was concerned.

Jumping in miserable conditions and ranked only seventh of the eight competitors, therefore only expected to gain 2 points at most, he immediately took the lead with a windy 7.89m - his best of the year. Commonwealth champion Eregbu for Africa wasn't far behind with 7.87m but with a second pass through the jumping order it was still the Belgrave man heading the field.

De Souza for the Americas came up with 7.96m in round 3 to push Fred down to 2nd but we were more than happy that his opening jump stood up through rounds 4 and 5 as the leading positions remained unchanged.

Surely no one in the stadium could have been prepared for what happened in the last round, however. Hitting the board just right and leaping into a slight headwind the Briton cut the sodden sand at a mega 8.10m causing the Crystal Palace crowd to erupt. It was the best jump of his life and the fourth best ever by a Briton. The American Bentley improved to 7.93m but that was it. Fred was the outstanding winner - eight points in the bag and what a way to prelude to his 30th birthday celebrations the following day.

* * *

Day two and more Belgrave action came from John Regis and Du'aine Ladejo. 1991 World Champ. USA's Antonio Pettigrew suddenly brought his 1994 season to life and coming out of the last turn he was 2 metres clear of Ladejo and in no mood to relinquish a single centimetre. It was the end of a tiring season for our Club Record holder - well not quite the end as there was the little matter of over an hour of autograph signing and then the next day's relay.

John was back to his old position of second bend man in the sprint relay. Britain had never previously won any relay in this competition but when Regis handed the baton to last leg man Christie the British team were already ahead of the field and going away. Would the currently injury prone Belgrave Harrier turn out for the 200m next day? With the Great British team slugging it out with Africa for the major honour of Champions of the World we suspected - yes.

And we were right. Once more we were treated to a Fredericks vs. Regis head to head. Fredericks had finished a stride clear in Madrid during the week but back on British soil the home town man hit the straight clearly ahead, wreaked revenge on Fredericks for that Commonwealth Games defeat and reminded Norwegian Moen who would have won the European Games if niggling injuries had not kept him out.

With Du'aine Ladejo contributing a 45.2 leg towards Britain's third relay win of the meeting it was quite staggering to work out that Belgrave athletes had contributed a massive 27 points towards the nation's outstanding score of 111 to Africa's 116.

200m (11th): -1.4. 1, J.Regis 20.45; 2, F.Fredericks (Africa) 20.55; 3, G.Moen (Europe) 20.72. 400m (10th): 1, A.Pettigrew (USA) 45.26; 2, D.Ladejo 45.44; 3, I.Sene (Americas) 45.67. LJ: (9th) 1, F.Salle (GBR) 8.10 (-0.2) (7.89w, x, 7.87, 7.82, x, 8.10); 2, D.De Souza (Americas) 7.96; 3, D.Bentley (USA) 7.93. 4x100m (10th): 1, GBR (D.Braithwaite, A.Jarrett, J.Regis, L.Christie) 38.46; 2, Africa 38.97; 3, USA 39.33. 4x400m (11th): 1, GBR (D.McKenzie, D.Ladejo, J.Baulch, R.Black) 3:01.34; 2, Africa 3:02.66; 3, Europe 3:03.26.

Flashback! - 1968

John Bicourt (30) takes the water jump in the Inter-Counties Championships of 1968. John finished 7th in 8:51.2; but five years later he became the UK's fastest 'chaser with 8:26.6. He finished 4th in the Commonwealth Games of 1974 (Christchurch, New Zealand). John's association with Kenyan athletes dates back further than many of us realise! Winner of that race back in 1968 was John Jackson (Staffs) in 8:38. Photo by Ed. Lacey

Report · page 24

Shaky start at Wigan — British Athletics League Division 1 Match 1, Wigan, 7 May

Men's Track & Field

Shaky start at Wigan

BRITISH ATHLETICS LEAGUE DIVISION 1 MATCH 1, WIGAN, 7 MAY

Every year we write to the 'A' team men twice before the start of the season. The message is always the same. The first match is the most important and invariably decides the final outcome of the league. Every point is critical and this year was to prove no exception as, after a fine match, only 13 points separated the first five clubs. Sadly we were 5th but all was not lost as main rivals Haringey also had problems and finished 3rd.

The match started well for us. Phil Spivey finished 3rd in the hammer having earlier in the week declared that he couldn't throw due to a hand injury. Scott Thompson won the 'B' - good points. The pole vault looked good but we had a worry that Sale and Crawley would raise the overall standard. So it proved with the main consolation for fewer than expected points being that Ian Tullett now seems recovered from his bone pinning operation.

The jumps we knew would not live up to last year's high points contribution. A combination of illness, warm weather training, injuries and other more pressing plans meant that Stanley Osuide was our only 'A' team regular. Partial saviour was Mark Richards who on no training stepped in at the last minute and scored well in the long, triple and high jumps - plus he ran first leg in the sprint relay.

The 800 and 1500 were like a Who's Who of star names. All races were slow with kick finishes. Darryl Maynard earned a Welsh vest in the subsequent Turkey match after doubling up while Youth Gavin Mason opened the season by equalling his 800 pb to the 1/100th sec.

AAA's indoor champ Phil Goedluck rushed back to his Fire Brigade night shift after putting in solid performances in all sprint races. Lewis Samuel doubled up in the 'B' 200 due to Lennie Paul's non-appearance and concluded an excellent afternoon with a 47.38 split in the long relay.

The throws were once again our saviour led by Paul Edwards. What a truly fantastic squad.

Last year's worst event was the 3000m steeplechase. No more! Darren Mead cruised round to a clear win acknowledging the need to pay more attention to his extravagant clearances of all barriers. Mark Sinclair suffered from the opposite problem, hitting a barrier at full speed on lap four while easily leading the 'B' pack. He gamely staggered on - went from first to last and 11 vital points went down the drain. He'll have to decide before next match whether to concentrate on the 'chase or take up limbo dancing!

Subsequently Darren doubled up in the 5000 and looked very relaxed, coasting to win the 'B' in a respectable 14:35. Kassa did the business in the 'A' 5000 gradually winding up before a good kick to cross the line for a clear win. The hurdles results were as expected. Paul Beaumont returned briefly from a posting in Germany to perform well in the 400 hurdles while Neil Owen was slightly below par due to travelling up from Cardiff on the day. The night before he had received a special award at Cardiff University so couldn't join the team at its pre-match hotel.

And so we entered the relays a close third. Sadly, due to all the doubling up and a shortage of sprinters two bad results saw us slip to 5th and possible oblivion! It doesn't take a genius to work out that just one more 'A' team man would have lifted us to 1st or 2nd. If only Dalton Powell and Haroun Korjie (both of whom supported the team from the stand) had been eligible to compete. They will be available for match two and could make a vital difference to keep us in contention.

Report · page 24

Southern League Division 1 Match 1 — Parliament Hill, 7 May

The first league match of the season - and the first utter soaking for officials and competitors alike. The pole vault was an early casualty, conditions being far too dangerous. But if the atmosphere was wet and cold, the competition was hot; we were up against some of the tougher clubs and by the end of the first round of matches throughout the South, Highgate, winners here, topped the league. Our own start was more modest but definitely better than last year's. We were 18th overall at the end of the day.

Mark Arend and Bill Silvester scored well to open for us in the 400 hurdles, and minutes later we were treated to a totally controlled 800 from Marcel Fok. Unfortunately we will soon be losing Marcel's services as he is due to move to a new job and home in Switzerland.

Our own human Alp, not very aptly going under the nomenclature of Mike Small, was not at his best. Mike moved through the curtains of rain from throw to throw but is suffering from a back complaint these days and was unable to do himself justice. The pick of our jumpers was Dr. Aham Amadi. He continues to defy anno domini and opened his 1994 season with a triple jump 13cm further than his best for last season.

Back on the track Gary Staines was one of only three winners for the Bels., a strong last lap in the 1500 getting him home ahead of a pack of good class club runners who were snapping at his heels down the straight. Roger Alsop ran to a pb for 'B' points in the same race. You know, he might just have gone under four minutes but for a slight lapse in the middle of the race.

Report · page 24

Southern League Division 5 Match 1 — Hayes, 7 May

Our third team, contesting the lower reaches of the league, is a most extraordinary outfit with team members' ages ranging from 17 to ... well ... into the 50s. Representing the younger element, Martin Readhead picked up our first win with a 'B' 800 victory. Triple jumper John Druce gave us win number two, good points from our Division Five Captain, but the event of the day was the 1500 metres where a back to form John Booth tore the heart out of the field for a winning margin of no less than 12 seconds.

Looking for 3 metres every time out, the father of the team, Terry Lawton, left the pole vault area under the impression that he had it won at 2.60. What he didn't know was that there was one final competitor still to come in at a height beyond his or any other competitor's comprehension!

Report · page 25

Rosenheim League East Division Match 1 — Battersea Park, 18 May

For the first time in a few years we entered the competition with a Rosenheim League team manager and the result was - a win! Four individual winners gave us a margin of victory which was fairly substantial but it has to be said that the backing up by making sure that we had every event covered was the key to victory. John Booth confirmed his fitness with an outstanding 1500m win and young Jamie Webb travelled all the way from the other side of Essex to chip in with full points in the vault. What a talent that Mark Richards has though; triple jump and 200m winner on no training - and precious little warm up either - it makes you sick.

Report · page 25

Southern League Division 1 Match 2 — Portsmouth, 4 Jun

We were short of officials and athletes for this one. Add to this the fact that it was bitterly cold with the rain falling almost constantly in sheets; we had no recognised throwers at all (they preferred to compete at Battersea in Division 5), Rob Cookson missed the start of the 800, Mike Bazire fell heavily when completing his 400m relay leg, no high jumpers or steeplechasers ... sometimes track & field seems such hard work. Luckily George Piddington showed up to lend a hand with the judging, it being his local track, and Lincoln Campbell's partner Sari sportingly agreed to help judge the jumps (and take the calls on Junior's mobile 'phone while he was racing). It was also good to see Gerry North buzzing around, preparing for the evening's road race.

Owain Lewes had clocked a 5k pb in the opening match and the rain here seemed to suit him as he tussled for the lead before falling away but still knocking a chunk more off his best. Captain Mike Bazire was everywhere "filling in" instead of concentrating on his favoured events and with wins from Gavin "Sunshine" Mason (would you believe it - he was the only one who managed to compete without getting a soaking), Aham Amadi, Neil Hammersley and Jamie Webb, we managed to avoid last place but dropped three more places to 22nd overall.

At home in the comfort of the warm and dry, thoughts about combining our S. League teams began surface. Reworking the scores, assuming that everyone in the Division 5 match would have been available to compete at Portsmouth, gave the surprising result that we would have finished exactly where we did - 4th! 100m: 3, L.Campbell 11.9; 3, M.Bazire 12.2. 200m: 4, L.Campbell 24.0; 3, M.Bazire 24.0. 400m: 1, G.Mason 49.9; 2, R.Cookson 52.0. 800m: 5, R.Draper 2:01.5. 1500m: 4, R.Alsop 4:02.9; 2, J.Booth 4:05.9. 5000m: 4, O.Lewes 15:45.2; 2, J.Galley 16:13.4. 110mH: 3, N.Hammersley 17.6. 400mH: 2, N.Hammersley 56.1. Report · page 26

Back from the brink — British Athletics League Division 1 Match 2, Copthall, 4 Jun

As we entered this match we felt that to stand any chance of final league success we would need to beat both Birchfield and Haringey. Newham & Essex Beagles were also a target as Sir Eddie had a season's side bet with their President and was losing after the first match. As it turned out we entered the final event of the day level with Thames Valley but whereas they finished 1st, we couldn't get any of our top 400 men to the start line and finished in last place. The match result fulfilled our key targets. We beat our main rivals to finish in 2nd place on the day but could progress no further up the league, remaining in 5th place but with the top bunching closer.

Highlights of the day were:- the first Club appearance of Mike 'Mad Dog' Morgan who won the long jump in impressive style; Haroun Korjie also in Belgrave colours for the first time and clocking an excellent 10.53; the same time to 1/100th for for Du'aine Ladejo and Phil Goedluck in the 'A' and 'B' 200 metres; Neil Owen's pb in the high hurdles, and the whole throws squad who once again pulled us back from the brink. 100m: 3, H.Korjie 10.53 (+0.9); 1, L.Paul 10.63 (0.0). 200m: 2, D.Ladejo 21.57 (+3.5); 1, P.Goedluck 21.57 (-0.5). 400m: 4, L.Samuel 48.32; 5, S.Shirley 49.60. 800m: 8, M.Fok 1:55.51; 6, J.Dawson 1:56.80. 1500m: 8, R.Turner 4:03.90; 2, G.Adams 4:03.64. 5000m: 1, K.Tadesse 14:28.50; 5, J.Estall 15:36.68. 110mH: 1, N.Owen 13.80 (w n/t); 6, L.Antoine 15.35. 400mH: 7, P.Beaumont 54.48; 7, I.Wells 55.31. 3000mSC: 8, M.Bizio 10:29.96. Report · page 26

AAA Championships — Birmingham, 11-12 Jun

The first day of the champs. saw Fred Salle lose his title to Cannock & Stafford's 39 year old Barrington Williams who found 7.77m good enough. Our own Mike Morgan put the pressure on Freddie as well. He led the competition by popping out his best jump of 7.59m in round one while the other two came up with their best leaps in round 6!

As usual, most of finals were scheduled for the second day and two of them went the way of Belgrave athletes. In the steeplechase the first two runners went under the European qualifying time with Justin Chaston 20m clear of Spencer Duval at the final water jump but only just holding him off as the line was reached. His time was a personal record and moved him a fraction closer to John Bicourt's now ancient club best of 8:22.82. Paul Edwards was over a metre clear of the rest to win the shot while a pb from came from Darren Mead in the 5k as he ran in to 2nd place.

Du'aine Ladejo just had the edge as favourite after the first two rounds of the 400 metres having set the fastest heat time of 46.50 and then running 1/100th faster than Roger Black in the semis with 45.82. When it came to the final, though, he hadn't bargain for such a powerful start from the old master whose 44.94 gave him a comfortable victory.

Another second place for the Bels came with Phil Goedluck's fine series of 200m runs. He started with 21.29 in the heat and then ran his first pb in the event since 1986 as he clocked a legal 20.90 in the semi-final. An 'over the limit' wind blew him to an even quicker 20.83 in the concluding race where Solomon Wariso got the better of him. a = 11/6, b = 12/6 100m: (a) s-f 3, L.Paul 10.57 (10.48ht); 5, P.Goedluck 10.49; hts 8, B.Williams 11.58. 200m: (b) (+3.2) 2, P.Goedluck 20.83. 400m: (b) 2, D.Ladejo 45.36; hts (a) 4, L.Samuel 47.65. 800m: hts (a) 7, D.Maynard 1:51.17. 5000m: (b) 2, D.Mead 13:54.08. 110mH: (b) 4, N.Owen 14.11. 400mH: hts (a) 6, P.Beaumont 54.59; 8, L.Antoine 56.36. 3000mSC: (b) 1, J.Chaston 8:28.28. Rosenheim League East Division Match 3 — Tooting, 15 Jun

It was so close. Five clubs all finished the evening within 8 points but with the opposition being up and down from one match to another the Bels were able to reap the reward of consistent performances by moving back into the position of league leaders, 2 points clear. The regular Wednesday rail strikes were making things difficult for all teams but with guys like Phil Carstairs who had ridden 30 miles on his bike to make sure that he could line up for the 3k, the ever-reliable Jamie Webb never missing an opportunity to take in another 'vaulting competition and reserves queuing up to fill in, we have a great mid-week outfit. Some of those non-scoring results deserve close attention: 3 metres from M50 Terry Lawton in the pole vault, Junior Galley and Said Chettati racing out every centimetre of the 3k together to finish within 2/10ths, and Dan Gomme and Martin Readhead winning their 400m races. 100m: 4, M.Dickson 11.7; n/s 5, A.King 12.4. 200m: 5, J.Dawson 24.2; n/s 2 3, M.Dickson 23.6; n/s 3 2, P.Fox 25.6. 400m: 2, G.Mason 49.6; n/s 1 1, D.Gomme 51.1; 4, R.Milner 52.8; 5, M.Fani 54.2; n/s 2 1, M.Readhead 53.8. 800m: 1, G.Adams 2:00.3; n/s 1, T.Dolman 1:57.8; 3, M.Anderson 2:04.5. 1500m: 1, R.Alsop 4:04.0. 3000m: 4, P.Carstairs 9:07.1; n/s 2, J.Galley 9:14.5; 3, S.Chettati 9:14.7; 14, T.Williams 10:22.4; 18, M.Manning 10:47.6. 110mH: 3, M.Holder 16.9. Report · page 27

Southern League Division 1 Match 3 — Woodford, 18 Jun

League ladders are funny things. Our Division One squad placed last here - and yet somehow moved up a place overall to 21st. Meanwhile our Division 'Fivers' at Crawley did well to notch up 3rd place with a small team - and dropped a league place as a result!

A great double win came from high jumpers Mark Arend and James Ganio, and Jamie Webb again did the business in the pole vault but once more the team had a makeshift look about it as we had to get people competing 'just for the point'; when did you last see Guy Sagar and Bob Bridges forming our 800m line-up?

We've always said that Junior Galley is more suited to middle distance track work rather than marathons. With his turn of speed he should be running more 5ks and even 1500 metres. Suddenly he seemed to have got the speed bug in a bad way, turning out in the 4x400 and even taking last leg in the 4x100 while our young sprinters and jumpers were off playing football at the other side of the park and missing the start! 100m: 4, L.Campbell 11.4; 3, M.Dickson 11.4. 200m: 4, L.Campbell 24.6; 2, M.Bazire 23.1. 400m: 4, M.Bazire 52.0; 2, W.Chiswell 53.3. 800m: 5, R.Bridges 2:40.6; 5, G.Sagar 2:55.6. 1500m: 2, R.Alsop 4:06.9; 3, P.Carstairs 4:33.6. 5000m: 3, P.Carstairs 16:21.6; 3, J.Galley 16:21.9. 110mH: 4, M.Holder 16.3. 400mH: 3, M.Arend 57.8. 3000mSC: 4, L.Lyons 10:34.1. Report · page 27

Southern League. Division 5 Match 3 — Crawley, 18 Jun

There were some good individual wins from the Bels but a dire shortage of sprinters in a closely fought match - only 9 points covered the first four teams at the end - meant that we had to settle for 3rd team. When you consider that we had no 100m runners at all and correspondingly no team in the 4x100m it's easy to see that it was at this match that we lost our battle to avoid relegation. A win here at Crawley would have kept us up.

Snowy Brooks managed to pack three-quarters of a decathlon into one afternoon and was delighted to win the 'A' long jump with his début as an M50 just a few weeks away. We have come to expect Snowy and Mike Small to turn the throws into high points earners but we also had a good pairing in the 'triple' where Kenny Pereira and John Druce picked up the maximum 10 points. Other good signs were the continuing metamorphosis of Alex Druce from jogger to serious track man, and the emergence of young talent prepared to have a go in senior competition in the form of James Drake. 200m: 4, K.Pereira 24.9; 5, D.Anderson 31.0. 400m: 1, C.Knight 52.8; 2, J.Drake 56.7. 800m: 4, J.Drake 2:10.3; 1, D.Anderson 2:18.8. 1500m: 2, A.Luce 4:18.2; 4, D.Anderson 4:48.6. 5000m: 5, T.Williams 18:25.1; 5, D.Anderson 19:05.2. 110mH: 3, C.Brooks 20.3; 4, J.Druce 24.5. 400mH: 1, C.Knight 60.5. 3000mSC: 1, J.Barry 11:06.4. Report · page 27

Rosenheim League East Division Match 4 — Croydon, 29 Jun

As our athletes gathered at Croydon Arena it became apparent that we were only a very small outfit at this unpopular venue. Team Manager Pat Mead had her work cut out to make sure that all events were covered throughout the match and had to use all her persuasive powers to get a 4x200 team to their marks for the last event. It was initially announced that we had placed 4th but the scoring was so tight that after checking it became apparent that we had in fact won by a single point with the first four clubs close up behind!

It underlines just how important everyone's contribution is. Take poor Snowy: he crashed badly at the third barrier in the 400 hurdles and lay on the track stunned until he heard someone call, "Go for it - the Belgrave man's fallen." That was like waving a red rag to a bull. Our 'iron-man' got up, staggered into action again and managed to cover the rest of the lap with blood running from his legs, now minus several square inches of skin. His two points made all the difference even though he was out of action for a fortnight.

Report · page 28

League Record from Regis but the 'Valley' pip Bels — British Athletics League, Division 1 Match 3, Linford Christie Stadium, 2 Jul

It was at this match that we finally realised that TVH represented the real threat to our league hopes. Their excellent strength in depth and a strong 4x400 squad was to be our undoing. In this match we actually entered the long relay one point in the lead but the Valley repeated their previous victory. Our squad was not disgraced as our youngest ever team finished 4th ahead of some classy opposition. Well done to Gavin Mason (17), Marcel Fok (20), John Dawson (20) and Ian Wells (don't ask) who ran a pb split of 49.1. We were just pipped by Haringey in the sprint relay - not surprising as they fielded Mensah, Jarrett, Callender and Wariso.

Michael Morgan repeated his long jump victory to put down a marker for the opposition and cleared 2 metres in the high jump to gain valuable points. John Regis won the 'A' 100 metres into a massive headwind and then ran 20.69 into a 1.7 mps wind to smash the league 200 metre record. His season suddenly seemed to be coming good. Paul Evans pleased all his fans by making a welcome appearance. Kassa tried to stay with him, blew up, and was pushed down into 2nd place in the 'B' - a bit different from his accustomed 'A' slot victory. Max Robertson's welcome return in the 'B' high hurdles gave good points but we frustratingly just missed out again. If only Haroun's blocks hadn't slipped in the 100 and if only Ian Tullett had managed a height in the pole vault. But that's the name of the game. If only.

Finally, a special mention Juniors Matthew Hillier and Wesley Swaffer who gained valuable points in the steeplechase. With their help we moved to a clear 2nd spot in the league. 100m: (-2.2) 1, J.Regis 10.55; (-1.3) 5, H.Korjie 10.99. 200m: (-3.3) 2, H.Korjie 21.43; (-1.7) 1, J.Regis 20.69. 400m: 7, G.Mason 48.89; 6, M.Edwards 51.50. 800m: 6, R.Turner 1:54.75; 5, M.Fok 1:54.28. 1500m: 6, R.Alsop 3:56.83; 7, R.Turner 4:14.91. 5000m: 1, P.Evans 14:24.49; 2, K.Tadesse 14:54.51. 110mH: (-0.1) 2, N.Owen 14.25; (-0.6) 1, M.Robertson 14.93. 400mH: 7, I.Wells 55.47; 4, L.Antoine 56.71. 3000mSC: 5, M.Hillier 10:17.07; 3, W.Swaffer 11:08.63. Report · page 28

Southern League Division 1 Match 4 — Newham, 2 Jul

Bob Bridges' hopes of a resurgence in our Southern League hopes were dashed when calls upon his squad by the British League team in the preceding hours had him shuffling the pack again; a re-deal resulted in no 400 metre runners or high jumpers. Still, we had some of our young stars in the team and particularly pleasing were the pbs from 15 year olds Marlon Dickson and Geoffrey Ojok. These two, together with Ben Cascoe, 16, and Mike Bazire, the 'old man' of the quartette, put together a fine 44.7 in the sprint relay. Geoffrey also put in some great jumping and a fastest ever 400 leg in the long relay.

Some of our other youngsters did not have such a good afternoon, however. James Ganio had the misfortune to turn his ankle on his very first long jump attempt, while James Hilston's back problem had him unable to compete at all.

A massive pb came from Junior Galley in 5k. He steadily became more confident as the race progressed and was boosted when he overtook club-mate and rival Owain Lewes who was suffering from the heat after going off with the leaders.

The performance of the afternoon, though, had to be the one set by Rob Draper - not in the 800, no, not the 1500 either. The high pressure hose used to fill the steeplechase water-jump was inadvertently dropped by the groundsman and seemed to take on the life of something from the Book of Revelations as it thrashed about spraying water over everyone within a 20m radius. If we didn't distinguish ourselves in the match at least we provided Rob, the man to wrestle with and hold down the soaking serpent while the tap was turned off

Report · page 28

Rosenheim League East Division Match 5 — Sutton Arena, 13 Jul

Yet another close match as Croydon hacked down our overall lead from 3½ points to 1½. We lost a few marks when the 'chase was cancelled due to the groundsman having failed to fill the water jump; poor old Marcello had made every effort to arrive early for it was well. Gavin Mason cruised a very comfortable two laps to give us our only scoring track win and then showed the enthusiasm that our young team exudes by filling in as a triple jumper!

Our other winner was Jamie Webb - but he was lucky. Not that he had any trouble clearing 3.70m in the pole vault, it's just that he competed in a non-club vest (with the judge's permission) and then had the meeting organiser querying his lack of 'claret and gold'. He got away with it but one has to be so careful. 100m: n/s (2) 3, M.Livingstone 12.4. 200m: 5, C.Husbands 25.6; n/s (1) 2, M.Livingstone 25.6; (2) 4, S.Smullen 26.9. 400m: 2, M.Parper 52.5; n/s 1, M.Readhead 54.5. 800m: 1, G.Mason 2:00.9; n/s M.Anderson 2:07.4; 2, A.Luce 2:08.3. 1500m: 2, R.Draper 4:10.7. 3000m: 2, P.Carstairs 9:10.8; n/s 1, S.Chettati 9:15.3; 6, M.Manning 10:28.9. Feature · page 28

Sensational year for "Tommy Green" Cup award

The "Tommy Green" Cup is awarded annually for the most meritorious performance by a male Belgrave athlete. As the results come in throughout the year members of the committee make mental notes of any performances that seem to fit the bill. 1994 was sensational. As Leo Coy said at the Club Dinner:

" When Kassa won the National Junior Cross Country I thought - that's it. But then Du'aine Ladejo triumphed in the Indoor Europeans and I wondered, no, that might be it. Then John Regis smashed the British 200 metre record and went below 20 seconds surely there was no doubt now - that's got to be it. But then Du'aine won the European outdoor 400 metre title! Finally Fred Salle got in on the act and leapt that amazing 8.10m in the World Cup Final to set the British team on an almighty high."

In the end the award went jointly to Fred Salle and Du'aine Ladejo.

Tommy GreenLeo CoyKassaDu'aine LadejoJohn RegisFred Salle

Report · page 29

Rosenheim League East Division Match 6 — Tooting, 27 Jul

We made it! By the skin of our teeth our men won their division of the 'Rosenheim' for the first time ever, earning 6 match points to take into the final although our margin of victory over Croydon was down to just half a point at the end. The standard of competition was particularly high, especially in the sprints where Hercules Wimbledon's 16 year-old Martin Giraud ran 10.8 and Herne Hill's White clocked a 21.8 - not bad for a mid-week league!

Chris Ogle made a welcome appearance in the team, taking the non-scoring 800m while Mr. Mason did the business in the match race. We were also strengthened by the showing of Ben Williams who took top points in the long jump. Rob Draper was looking for a fast race in the 1500m but got more than he bargained for as the leaders toured the route in well under 4 minute pace.

As darkness gathered there was some doubt about whether we had actually pulled off the overall victory and the last event, the 4x200 relay, added even more excitement to the situation. Michael Parper, Ben Williams and Gavin Mason got the baton to Frazer Hazlett in the lead but with the opposition queuing up to run him down. The Croydon man reached him first and they raced the final stretch with nothing between them as Giraud pulled them both back hand over fist. At the line Frazer just had enough of a lean to get the verdict although Croydon were given an identical time with H/W 2/10ths in arrears. Report · page 29

Southern League Division 1 Match 5 — Basildon, 30 Jul

Traffic conditions were diabolical as our team made their way to Essex for the penultimate match. Mike Small arrived with the third round of the hammer already under way, dashed to the hammer cage - and dumped one out just far enough to get into the final six, thus earning three more efforts. With his adrenaline now pumping our big man then put the ball and chain out to his best distance in years.

Jamie Webb had no such problems, this being his side of the country, but he was also on a 'high'. Having already set a pb in the 'vault, he had the bar set at 4 metres and was delighted with yet another clearance.

On the track our day started well as Neil Hammersley put the opposition well behind him in the 400m hurdles - but almost immediately our team was shown to have flaws as we failed to turn out a 'B' runner. Still, we were much stronger in the middle-distances than normal.

As Helsinki bound Gary Staines lined up for the 800 he was described by the announcer as "a flash sort of guy". But there's a lot of difference between the 25 laps of Gary's specialist distance and the two circuits demanded here - especially when the league throws up athletes that delight in 'having a pop' at a 'name'. The Belgravian made a mistake in not turning up the pace early enough and paid for it when two of the opposition outkicked him over the last 200m. No such mistake in the 1500, however, as a last lap of 55 seconds destroyed anyone's hopes of doing it again. Graham Adams picked up winning points in both 'B' races.

Ben Williams was around 7 metres with marginal fouls in the long jump and with Aham Amadi showing him how to do it, won his first ever triple jump, with his tutor keeping just far enough in front to take the 'A'. Talking of Aham, he had turned up without his trainers but as a true team manager Bob went barefoot for the rest of the afternoon.

Another man with his sights on a major games was decathlon man Simon Shirley. Report · page 29

THE GREAT ESCAPE! — Guardian Gold Cup Semi-Final, Kingsmeadow, 31 Jul

Entry to the Cup Final is from four semi-finals with the first two teams of each match progressing. An element of seeding takes place in that the first four teams in the previous year's final are seeded into separate matches. As these are invariably division one clubs it is theoretically possible to have one of the semis with five division one clubs (one of the top four plus the remaining four division one clubs). The odds on this happening are enormous. Well Belgrave had a home fixture ( at Kingsmeadow) which included four division one clubs so we knew we would need a strong ride to progress further.

The selection from available members looked strong enough. All that we needed was an early morning trip to Gatwick to collect those who had competed in Belgium the night before and we were on our way. Well, we weren't. A plane had blocked the single runway and flights were delayed for four hours. Back at Kingsmeadow panic was setting. There were no reserves for the pole vault so Mike Edwards Snr. was press-ganged into action with borrowed kit and pole. John Mackenzie stood in for Mike Morgan in the long jump and Mike repaid the favour in the triple jump, his first and last triple jump competition ending in injury. After six events we were trailing in 6th place. Paul Beaumont had driven back from Germany for the 400 hurdles and it showed as he trailed home in 7th spot. Abi Ekoku did well to finish 3rd in his first shot for two years but the 800, 10,000 and steeplechase saw our hopes tumbling only to rise again as Simon Shirley dead-heated in the high hurdles. Almost immediately afterwards Darrin Morris won the discus, Kassa won the 5000 and Nigel Bevan eased to a clear win in the javelin. Darren Mead ran the best 1500 by a Belgravian for the year, finishing a close 3rd. Silver for Haroun in the 200m and double wins in the relays saw us safely home. But once again the team managers were looking like limp rags.

Report · page 30

Bels win the "Rosenheim" after a 30 year wait — Rosenheim League Final, Tooting, 17 Aug

When Rosenheim League founder Jim Braben presented team manager Pat Mead with the Rosenheim Plate for our win in the Eastern Division, plus a brand new trophy for winning the Final, news-hound Pete Mulholland couldn't help commenting, " ... thirty years before Belgrave could win it ... and it's taken a woman to bring it off!" Not only did we win it - but it was a record score as well, as some of our heavyweights joined in the action.

Pat was quick to point out, though, that the success was really due to the regular support of the younger middle-order competitors and the fact that we had plenty of non-scorers to step into the breach whenever required; guys like Martin Readhead, Marlon Dickson, etc. A good example was Adam Armstrong. He didn't actually score in any of the events throughout the summer but was usually there offering to do anything to make sure we didn't lose a point. On holiday in Northern Ireland while the final was on, his first knowledge of our success came when he purchased a copy of "AW" in an Irish newsagent's. His yelp of, "Yes" and clenched fist while still in the shop must have had other shoppers wondering what was going on.

Speed man Dalton Powell was still around and scored well for us but what super backing he got as young Belgrave sprinters won each of the following non-scoring events and filled positions 1 to 4 in the first n/s 200m. Our 4x200 squad ran to the country's 6th fastest time of the year and we had one and a half non-scoring teams as well.

Rob Turner gave us an 800 win while Abi Ekoku and Phil Spivey joined in the fun on the field. It was Abi's last competiton for us before signing up for a season with the London Broncos.

Well, we set out our aim at the beginning of the season and our campaign paid off for the 30th Anniversary of the League. Can we do it again in '95? There'll be a few clubs out there intending to make sure that we don't! 100m: 2, D.Powell 11.5; n/s (1) 1, M.Dickson 11.9; (2) 1, B.Williams 11.9; 2, R.David 12.0. 200m: 1, D.Powell 22.2; n/s (1) 1, G.Palmer 22.5; 2, R.David 23.2; 3, B.Williams 23.2; 4, M.Bazire 23.6; (2) 1, J.Dawson 23.6; 3, M.Readhead 24.7. 400m: 3, G.Mason 50.1; n/s 1, D.Gomme 51.0; 2, M.Parper 52.5. 800m: 1, R.Turner 1:57.5; n/s (1) 4, S.Smullen 2:07.6; (2) 1, J.Hilston 2:11.9. 1500m: 3, A.Luce 4:18.8; n/s 1, J.Galley 4:17.0; 2, J.Wilson 4:27.8. 3000m: 2, R.Alsop 8:41.7. 2000mSC: 6, P.Carstairs 7:39.7. Notice · page 31

Physiotherapy for £5 at Tooting

Did you know that you can receive physiotherapy treatment for as little as £5! If you have a problem there is no excuse for not getting it treated. The Club has joined with Herne Hill Harriers and Hercules Wimbledon AC in paying for a physiotherapist to attend Tooting Track every Tuesday night from 6.30pm until 8.30pm. All you have to do is take along your Club Rule Book or some other proof that you are a Belgrave member and you will be treated for just £5.

Report · page 32

Young Athletes' Track & Field

NATIONAL JUNIOR LEAGUE DIVISION 3, MATCH 1 TONBRIDGE, 17 APR

Now in the depths of winter, writes Toby Earle, I'll reminisce about happier, warmer, sunnier times ... but unfortunately I have to start with the meeting at Tonbridge where it was cold, wet and windy. The weather was as unpleasant then as it is now - hardly a ray of sunshine all day - and the team performance was no better. I suppose that's slightly unfair as the team consisted of about twelve people, so we couldn't have really expected to do very well. Reasons for the poor turnout ? In the morning there had been the London Mini-Marathon but apart from that it seemed as if everyone else was in hibernation until the weather improved.

As most of the track athletes have solar panels to trap and use the sun's energy there were not many exceptional peformances ... but there were a few. For example, Simon Weston's 800m was a really good run; he clocked 60.0 on his first lap and then continued to pull away. But there were other incidents that showed it was not going to be Belgrave's day, the most memorable being the 4x100m relay in which we were disqualified at two of the three changeovers!

We ended up a measly fifth out of six teams with effectively all promotion chances knocked on the head - our main aim for the season already in tatters. 100m: 6, A.King 12.4. 200m: 4, M.Dickson 23.9. 400m: 1, G.Mason 51.1; 3, J.Hilston 52.6; n/s D.Sleet 60.2. 800m: 2, M.Readhead 2:08.4; 1, S.Weston 2:03.2. 1500m: 2, W.Swaffer 4:37.3. 3000m: 2, M.Readhead 10:04.8. 110mH: 1, M.Holder 17.2. 2,000mSC: 3, W.Swaffer 7:03.6; 1, T.Earle 7:26.6. NATIONAL YA LEAGUE SOUTHERN PREMIER DIVISION MATCH 1 BATTERSEA PARK, 1 MAY

We had some great individual performances at our home McDonalds match but the standard in the Premier Division is very tough and overall we were well last. But let's take a look at a few of those individual efforts.

Michael Parper had a fantastic afternoon as he strung together a 50.1 individual 400m, a 2:02.8 800m and a 50.7 400m leg in the long relay - all pbs and not surprisingly he was voted track man of the match.

Not to be outdone, however, was Ben Cascoe whose winning javelin throw earned him the field events man of the match award and at last destroyed Peter Crosbie's 20 year old Club Record. The old mark of 54.62 had eluded him by just 4cm last year but with the 1994 season hardly started it looked as if 60m was a possibility before long.

Super triple jumping came from James Ganio and James Hilston and Marlon Dickson was looking really hot in the sprints in his first year in the age group. NATIONAL YA LEAGUE SOUTHERN PREMIER DIVISION MATCH 2 MAYESBROOK PARK, 22 MAY

If we had any hopes of improving our league position they were blown away in the wintry squalls at Mayesbrook Park where a lot of things seemed to go wrong. Take Michael Parper for instance - three weeks ago he was on the verge of going sub-50 but here he fell in the 400m while over-reaching for the line and then 'blew up' in the 800m.

Es Hassan made a great impression in the U15s 100m/200m and wasn't far off a hat trick as he leaped to 2nd in the long jump. We had a double win in the U13 800m where Simon Fuller ran one of the most laid back races ever seen; running along in last place to start with, every time the man in front of him started to tail off the pace he moved up a position - until coming into the straight for the last time Simon took lead. The distance events were well taken care of. John Wilson and Simon Alexander ran strongly in the U17 3k and Simon Vella had no problems in the U15 1500m.

In fact, in the upper age-group we were as strong as any team but our younger lads were giving away years, many of them being eligible for the same age group for a good while to come yet and that's the sort of disadvantage that is difficult to overcome. Their time will come! SEAA U20 CHAMPS. CRYSTAL PALACE 28/29 MAY

Our top medallist at the Southern Championships was Es Hassan, competing in the under 15 group and taking home two gold medals by winning the 200 metres and long jump.

Gavin Mason put a disappointing 800 metre performance in Cyprus behind him by racing to a pb by nearly two seconds. In Nicosia for the World Schools Games he had clocked 1:56.65 but did not progress to the final. Back in Britain however, he was in the medals and still has two more years in the same age group.

Another best ever came from James Hilston in the under 17 400 metres. Several of the opposition set faster times in the heats but the rangy young Belgravian, still only lightly trained, shaved his previous best set indoors in February. Behind him was Epsom's Steve Mitchell, victor in the County Champs with a sub-50 clocking.

A gutsy performance came from Rob Milner in the long hurdles race. Rob has hardly had the best of preparation for the '94 season, what with appendicitis and shin splints, but his 56.39 here not only gave him 3rd place, it reduced his Club Record by virtually one whole second. And talking of Club Records, Ben Cascoe upped his 700gm Javelin mark by another 62cm. U20M 800m: 3, G.Mason 1:53.60. U17M 400m: 3, J.Hilston 50.56; 6, M.Parper 52.09. 400mH: 3, R.Milner 56.39. NATIONAL JUNIOR LEAGUE DIVISION 3, MATCH 2 ALDERSHOT, 12 JUN.

Toby Earle reports. Well, as the cliche states, surely things could only get better. The weather at Aldershot was warm and sunny but not even the fair weather could coax more athletes into action. Once again we were few in number and could only loosely be termed a "squad". This time the numbers were low due to the county schools being held the previous day, and if we didn't lose athletes to that event then they were injured. I fell into the latter category (turning my attentions from the track onto the field) and so too did Rob Milner. Rob had fallen badly during the previous day's Surrey Schools 400mH, but he soldiered on. The biggest surprises of the day were Geoffrey Ojok's performances - he had never thrown a javelin before but finished third in that event and also ran a brilliant leg in the 100m relay in flat shoes.

Our final standing was exactly the same as at Tonbridge; fifth out of six but only two points adrift from fourth place. Things must get better, musn't they ? 100m: 4, M. NATIONAL YA LEAGUE SOUTHERN PREMIER DIVISION MATCH 4 DEANGATE, 26 JUN

This was more like it. We filled out more of the events and the under 13 team was boosted by the first appearance of sprinter Moses Semakula - a great prospect. With Marlon Dickson, James Hilston, Geoffrey Ojok, Es Hassan and the Pauls Fox and Hills all winning their sprints, it was not surprising that we also did well in the relays. The under 17 4x100m quartet actually broke our ten year old Club Record with a 44.7 timing - until they found that they had been disqualified for running out of lane at one point.

Again we provided a Man of the Match in Rob Milner, for his 58.0 400m hurdles run and Captain Steven Scott, much more at home over sprint hurdles, played a real Captain's role by offering to do the 1500m steeplechase as well - pity he sprinted for the line a lap too early to turn it into a really painful episode! NATIONAL YA LEAGUE SOUTHERN PREMIER DIVISION MATCH 5 PETERBOROUGH, 17 JUL

And so to the last match of the series. We were lying 14th out of the 16 clubs involved in the Southern Premier Division and needed to win to have a snowball in hell's chance of staying up. However, in Blackheath (winners for the previous 9 years) and Borough of Enfield we were up against two of the toughest sets of opponents throughout the whole Mcdonald's League and in our heart of hearts we knew it was going to be impossible.

Unfortunately, in spite of repeated attempts, we have been unable to obtain a set of results from the organisers but we can point out a couple of the outstanding events as far as we were concerned.

Marlon Dickson was up against 10.88 man Mark Findlay and not surprisingly had his work cut out in the individual 100m. The sprint relay was something else! Ben Cascoe, Geoffrey Ojok and James Hilston had built up a 6 metre lead to give Marlon an outside chance of holding on to beat the 'Heathens'. And for 98 metres of that last leg we thought he was going to pull off a famous victory but that Findlay is a powerful man and he just got his chest ahead of the Belgrave runner before the line. But at last our under 17 Club Record had gone ... by 7/10ths of a second ... and no take-over infringements either.

One of the members of that 1984 relay team was Clive Gardner and he was lucky to keep his name on the under 17 record books at all because Steve Scott matched his 100m hurdle record time identically. Well done Captain. Admittedly there was a bit of a following breeze but in inter-club competition we rarely have the luxury of a wind gauge (who is to say Gardner's run was not aided by a gale?) so all such times without a wind reading must go down on the record lists. NATIONAL JUNIOR LEAGUE DIVISION 3 MATCH 3 FELTHAM, 24 JUL

With our under 20 men's team bolstered by many of our outstanding under 17s, for the first time we showed what we are capable of in this league. The standard of competition was really high but we got within a mere 4 points of carrying the day. In fact, going into the relays it was announced that we had a 1 point advantage over Borough of Hounslow and Team Manager Leo Coy had a frantic few minutes pulling athletes back from all over the arena to make sure that he could field our strongest squad. We won them both - so how the final scores showed us just out of 1st place we'll never know.

Richard David was back from injury, although still just a little wary of his hamstring, and it was great to have him back in the team. Marcus Miller strengthened our jumps and hurdles events and we owe so much to guys like Martin Readhead who are prepared to have a go at everything from triple jump to 3k.

Report · page 34

100 miles in 12 hours relay challenge

One of Steve Whorlow's group's fundraising activities has been their 100 miles in 12 hours attempt. As well as raising funds for travel, there was also the challenge to complete the task. In 1993 they completed 96¾ miles in the 12 hours, being hampered by another event being held in the park on the same day.

March 1994 was another story. Eighteen runners aged from 8 to 18 years assembled at 8am and the challenge began. Each athlete took turns to run one lap of Radnor Park (measured by the R.R.C. as being 945 metres). This meant nearly 171 laps during the day. By 6.30pm the task was nearly over and at 6.43pm the 100 miles was complete.

The challenge now goes out to any group of up to twenty five youngsters to travel to Folkestone in March and compete against them, to better this time. The average age must be 13½years.

The 1994 individual best times were: U17 Bobby Peacock, 2:48 (12th lap), U15 Nigel Whorlow, 3:26 (10th lap), U13 Jason Stevens, 3:26 (9th lap). Each athlete completed 14 or 15 laps. Those competing were Robert Bignall (12), Paul Price (12), Stephen Whorlow Jnr (8), Jason Stevens (12), Danny Crook (12), Nigel Whorlow (12), Paul Knight (12), Toby Laws (13), Bobby Peacock (16), Nathan Scamp (15), Wesley Swaffer (16), Lee Jenner (16), Chris Wilson (15), Trevor Santer (16), Amy Butler (14) and Katherine Phillips (13).

Report · page 36

TSB County Schools C.C. Champs. '94

Parliament Hill was the setting for the '94 London Championships where our best performer was Simon Vella, romping home to win the Junior Boys' event. Jessica Dash led her Lycee Francais team to victory in the Senior Girls' race but is not a great lover of cross-country and did not take up her opportunity to run for London in All-England. Senior Boys 6, M.Anderson 24:33. Inter Boys 5, J.Wilson 22:06; 31, M.Parper. Junior Boys 1, S.Vella 13:42. Senior Girls: 7, J.Dash 18:26. Junior Girls: 2, L.Wilson 11:54.

At Reigate in the Surrey Champs. we had three Belgrave runners in the first eight in the Senior Boys' race. Matt Hillier and Adam Jones gave South Surrey a great start but it was Gavin Mason's Central Surrey team that took the winners' trophy. Senior Boys 3, M.Hillier 27:16; 5, A.Jones 27:32; 8, G.Mason 27:50; 17, S.Walsh; T.Earle also ran. Inter Boys 9, S.Alexander 20:53; J.Drake and S.Bramson also ran. Ben Hamill ran in the Middlesex race but we don't know his position, while Gavin Jones came home 7th Inter Boy in 18:28 in the Sussex event at Brighton.

Simon Vella won again when it came to the Schools Inter-Counties at Southampton on February 12th. Running for London his margin of victory was four seconds in the Junior Boys' 4.8k race won in 14:56. Of the Junior Girls' Laura Wilson placed 11th in the 3k event, also for London, in 13:20.

Simon Alexander was 2nd under 16 in the 2.75k Croydon Advertiser schools race at Lloyd Park on March 5th in 16:17.

Meanwhile, up at Sefton Park, Liverpool, the English Schools CC Champs. were taking place with six Belgravians taking part. Sen. Boys' 8k 126, G.Mason (Sry) 29:12; 147, M.Hillier (Sry) 29:25; 200, A.Jones (Sry) 29:57. Inter Boys' 6.5k 237, G.Jones (Ssx) 23:43. Jun Boys' 5k 6, S.Vella (Lon) 16:28. Jun Girls' 3.5k L.Wilson (Lon) 13:59.

Report · page 36

Indoor records for Cockram and Owen

The Puma Open meetings at Crystal Palace gave the chance for a couple of Belgravians to shine in spite of the winter gloom. On January 14th Neil Owen three times ran a Club Record 8.1 over the 60 metre hurdles and Julia Cockram cleared 2.80 in the pole vault to equal Susan Drummie's indoor Club Record. Neil also ran 7.3 for the flat 60m on the 11th. John Mackenzie and Mike Morgan have also been in action, turning in 7.26 and 7.22 efforts on January 14th at Woodford.

Results · page 37

Schools results

CENTRAL SURREY CHAMPS 19 May, Kingsmeadow

Belgrave swept the board on the track with winners in the 400m to 1500m plus steeplechase. Some great triple jumping came from James Hilston - although he was later to pay for it with a persistant knee problem. Simon Weston was particularly impressive in the 800m, going it alone with a 57 second first lap and continuing for an aggravating final clocking of 2 minutes dead - a championship best. Surrey at Kingsmeadow

The hard luck story of the champs was that of 400 metre hurdler Robert Milner. A possible winner, he over rotated at a hurdle going into the back straight and fell badly, lying on the deck for a considerable length of time before recovering his composure and getting up to finish a battered and disappointed 4th.

The senior boys' 800 saw last year's leading inters Toby Dolman (Crawley AC and now 2nd-claim Belgrave) and Gavin Mason matching strides again. In '93 it was Toby who got to the line first by a whisker. In this year's clash an initial circuit of 55 seconds by Gavin was not enough to shake off his rival but lengthening his stride into the back stretch, daylight began to open and his final 1:53.8 lopped a pleasing chunk off his pb. TSB ENGLISH SCHOOLS CUP FINAL

2 July. Sheffield Inter Boys PV: 7, A.Hill (Dulwich) 2.40.

TSB ENGLISH SCHOOLS CHAMPS 8-9 Jul. Telford

This year we had eight at the championships but although there were some good performances from our young Bels., we nevertheless came away from Telford without a winner.

In his first year as an intermediate, James Hilston ran his fastest ever (50.15) in the heats, ensuring progression to the next round by chasing home Slocumbe of Dorset who clocked 49.94. The first five were all under 51 - super running from 15 and 16 year-olds. Another hard run came in the semis where James (50.21) was up against Slocumbe again (49.76) and not surprisingly it was the Dorset man who eventually took the final in 49.71. Baird of Humberside was 2nd in 49.96 while James in 3rd beat the only other man to have broken 50 during the competition. Can we expect 48 and bits from James in '95? Marlon Dickson ran an 11.58 for 2nd in his heat - into a 2.0 wind! Later he was able to take advantage of a following breeze to get down to 11.36 in final. And great sprinting also came from Es Hassan in the 200m; he ran 23.9 against a 2.6 wind to win his heat and then went to 23.34 (+3.4mps) and 23.30 (+5.9) as the wind direction changed.

Gavin Mason disappointingly only got up to 5th in the opening heat of the 800m. His time, within 7/10ths of the winner, would have won any of the other heats. Michael Parper had earlier been our long shot for an intermediate win after running 50.1/1:59 in early season competition but he was short of training after a knee problem, while Rob Milner did well to reach the 400 hurdles semis after health troubles earlier in the year. Senior Boys 800m: s-f 8, T.Dolman (Surrey) 1:59.26 (1:55.87 ht); hts 5, G.Mason 1:54.91. Young Belgravians

Captions to photographs on the next two pages:

Top row (left to right): Laura Wilson on her way around two laps during the Women's Southern League match at Perivale. She clipped her own U15G 800m Club Record by 1/10th in 1994, taking it to 2:26.0.

Matthew Coleman races for the line during one of the wheelchair races at the Copthall British League match.

Rebecca Brown, Shelley Skippen, Abigail Gray and Laura Walden pose between events at Perivale.

Pictured racing over 200 metres for a change, at Aldershot in the National Junior League, Rob Milner took nearly a whole second off his 400m hurdles Club Record in 1994 with 56.39.

Centre: Robert Bignall , under 13 Boys' Cross Country Captain, is just as determined when it comes to track racing.

John Wilson and Simon Alexander both scored well for us in the McDonald's League distance races; on this occasion, at Deangate, they both went well under 10 minutes for the 3,000 metres..

Bottom: Undeterred by the shattered hurdle in the next lane at the first flight of barriers, Captain Steve Scott went on to equal our ten year old Club Record of 14.3 for the U17M 100m hurdles.

Surely U15B Es Hassan must have scored more points than anyone during summer. An All-England Schools 200 metre finalist, he ended the season with pbs at 11.85, 23.59 and 54.09 in the sprints, and 5.75 in the long jump. He took three gold medals and one silver in the Surrey County T&F Champs.

Simon Weston and Gavin Jones took 2nd and 1st places respectively in the 'A' and 'B' U17M 800 metre races at Deangate.

And what a talent this young man has. Moses Semakula has been taking the U13B sprints by storm. Moses won A.A.A. Grade I Awards in 100m, 200m and shot putt during the summer, the latter with a Club Record distance of 9.77.

All photos by Alan Mead

Women's Track & Field

WOMEN'S SOUTHERN LEAGUE DIVISION 3 MATCH 1 WORTHING, 23 APR

The cinder track took most of our team by surprise - some of them didn't even seem to have seen one before. They should have been here a generation earlier when all tracks were cinder except a few like this one at Worthing which was then grass!

Sutton & District were the revelation of the match. Newly promoted from Division 4, they surprised even themselves by starting their 1994 campaign where they left off last year - with a win. We were pleased enough with our 4th place among some good clubs, being very short through illness and expecting to come last.

Among the highlights were a long jump pb from Syreeta Stracey and an exciting come-back by Jacinta Moore in the 1500m after being some 30m off the pace. Pam Friel was well in command in the 400m and given still conditions and a few more races under her belt looked set fair for a sub-60 clocking in the next few weeks. It was interesting to note that our senior 100 metres pair were both daughters of our lady veterans' team 100m runners - a family sport this athletics. WOMEN'S SOUTHERN LEAGUE DIVISION 3 MATCH 2 PARLIAMENT HILL, 21 MAY

Plenty of excitement came our way as we got yet another soaking at this North London venue. Does it always rain at Parliament Hill?

The coach very nearly got stuck while manœuvreing through the narrow entrance, a para-medic helicopter landed next to the track in mid-competition, the tent sheltering our shivering team got all but blown away and, oh! yes, we won the match! Jessica Dash and Jackie Stone set the afternoon up with double wins in the 400m hurdles but although we had several other winners and a good sprinkling of 2nds the result was in doubt until the last relay was over. Gail Richardson was pleased with her javelin win and although Julia Cockram must have been just as happy with her maximum score in the high jump, there is surely a more enjoyable way to spend an afternoon than Fosbury flopping onto one's back into a three inch deep puddle in the middle of the landing bed.

Gabby Collison had a rare excursion over 800m but this was just the warm up for a battle royal with international road runner Danielle Sanderson over 1500m. The Watford runner had also had a loosener earlier on - over 3k - and there was nothing in it for the first three laps. But the Belgravian is showing a fair old turn of speed these days and when the pace was upped for the run home it was the claret vested runner who won the day. Just as exciting, but for different reasons, was the 4x100m where we went into the last takeover ahead of a closely packed field. Along the straight the whole field evened up until they crossed the line with virtually nothing between them. A difficult one for the judges (which included our own George Piddington representing Havant) and on this occasion it didn't go our way as we had gone from 1st to 5th right on the line.

We must make a special mention of our under 15 girls. Only seven in number and four of these all the way from Folkestone, we couldn't have won the match without them.
