WOMEN'S SOUTHERN LEAGUE DIVISION 3 MATCH 3 SUTCLIFFE PARK, 4 JUN

It was back to 4th team place once more at Sutcliffe Park in yet another rain soaked match. Again a double 400m hurdle win from gave us a good start and we didn't drop a single point over 1500 and 3000 metres as we turned out the super quartet of Helen Maskrey, Nina Mills, Morag Andrew and Nikki Haines. But there were a couple of gaps in the team where we had no-one to line up with the opposition - and that's all it takes.

A Club Record was set by Laura Wilson in the under 15 800 metres. She played with the opposition before striding away to reduce her best ever to 2:26.0 - 1/10th below the mark she set last year. Over one lap Pam Friel also improved her best - by 6/10ths to a tantalising 60.2.

Another Club best came from Petronell Keeler, now getting to grips with the hammer and two others who tackled brand new events (for them) were Julia Cockram in the 400m and Hazel Mead in the 80m hurdles. Once a team manager sees you do something new, you're down on the books as a likely reserve for ever! WOMEN'S SOUTHERN LEAGUE DIVISION 3 MATCH 4 PERIVALE, 16 JUL

At last the sun came out for what turned out to be a blistering afternoon. The home club were at full strength and having recently amalgamated with Middlesex Ladies proved that they are going to be a force in WSL athletics. As far as the Bels were concerned, our team was just that bit stronger - and needed to be to gain us a 3rd team place which took us to 11th overall.

Having already won the Guardian Jubilee Cup 1500m in a personal best, Helen Maskrey now took another whopping eight seconds off and is really beginning to look a track runner as she tucked in with the leading bunch, pushed the pace along at the leader's shoulder, and then stretched away over the last 300m for a clear win.

As normal Angela Patten popped up everywhere - jumps, throws - it's all the same to her, and Gail Richardson also scored well with wins in the discus and hammer. It was good to have Jacqui Smiter back in the team after months of problems with shin splints and we welcomed two new under 15s to bolster our younger section - thrower Michelle Stock and sprinter Melissa Bentt. WOMEN'S SOUTHERN LEAGUE DIVISION 3 MATCH 5 CARN BRAE, 13 AUG

Ten hours in a coach is long enough for anybody and don't forget, some of the team had been up since dawn to get from Folkestone to London! It was with relief that we passed through the granite entrance way of Henry VIII's Pendennis Castle, our "home" for the next couple of nights.

Away matches to Cornwall really are a long way away but we weren't the only London team to have made the long haul for in our competition we came up against Richmond & Twickenham and in the men's Southern League held in conjunction Thames Valley were in action. The biggest cheer of the afternoon came before the match had got under way - when we were welcomed as the club for which new European Champion Du'aine Ladejo competes.

Our best performances came in the throws where under 15s Michelle Stock and Tigho Ejoh came up with double wins in the shot and discus. These two are going to be great assets in the years to come. Rhiann Letang set a pb in the javelin and with Melissa Bentt and Laura Wilson also winning in this age group, for the first time in a long while it was the seniors who struggled most to score points.

Not surprisingly the local club were by far the strongest. But 3rd place for the Bels, getting on for 300 miles from home, gave us plenty to celebrate - and celebrate we did with "Fish & Chips for forty please" and an impromptu party on the beach. WOMEN'S SOUTHERN LEAGUE DIVISION 3 MATCH 6 BATTERSEA PARK, 3 SEP

Back on our home ground to finish off the season we came so close to another win. As it was, we had to be satisfied with a 2nd place that took us to a creditable final 9th place in the league. With things looking stronger in the younger age-group and some good winter training we could be shooting for an even higher position next year.

We managed to round up eleven under 15s - our best for the summer - and they scored well with our strong girls Michelle and Tigho again in good form. Rhiann Letang is another reliable competitor; she has competed in every Southern League match this season, usually taking in three or four events in an afternoon.

Among the seniors we saw a hammer record from Petronell Keeler, Angela Abrams getting to the front of the 100 metres field and some very strong performances by the middle distance runners. Star of the track had to be Gabby Collison, however. Her 1500 metres win was absolutely devastating. She is now four seconds faster than last year and well within reach of Hilary Baxter's classy record of 4:31.4 set in 1981. Report · page 45

Belgrave Open '7', Wimbledon, 26 Nov

The Belgrave Open '7' is always a time when old friends meet up and view the passing of the years. The 1994 version of the event was special even by these terms, however. It was the 75th holding of the event and 1964 Olympic 20k Champion Ken Matthews attended as guest of honour, presenting the prizes and adding lustre to the proceedings. Athletes from all over the country came along to compete, Athletics Weekly provided a little in the way of sponsorship and instead of the normal "break even" on the finances, race organiser Carl Lawton was able to to show a handsome profit at the end of the day - 9 pence - subject to audit of course!

Manx Harrier Steve Partington was the dominant force as far as the race was concerned. He immediately took the initiative, chased by Plymouth's Chris Maddocks and, for a while, Chris Cheeseman of Surrey. There was no doubting Maddocks' determination to hang on as the race progressed but a small gap was mercilessly levered to become a bigger one until Partington eventually crossed the line in Cottenham Park Road with a 50 seconds advantage, well under Mark Easton's inaugural record of 1993.

A 2 kilometre event was also held for the younger age-groups and Steve Whorlow's lads and lasses made a good showing for the Bels. with Laura Fryer recording the home club's only win of the day. 1, S.Partington (Manx) 47:57 (rec); 2, C.Maddocks (Plymouth) 48:47; 3, C.Cheeseman (Surrey WC) 50:40; ... 6, J.Hall 55:22; 19, D.Sharpe 61:49; 22, P.King 63:20; 27, D.Fotheringham 64:22. Teams: 1, Steyning 40; 2, Belgrave 74; 3, Surrey WC 76. Women: 1, K.Horwill (D&S) 61:25. Teams: Steyning 19. 2k U15B: 1, M.Hayles (Steyning) 9:46; 3, N.Whorlow (Belgrave) 13:19. U13B: 1, D.King (Colchester J) 11:06. U11B: 1, B.Pullen (Lough) 13:19; 2, R.Bignell (Belgrave) 13:35; 3, S.Whorlow Jnr. 15:31. U11G: 1, L.Fryer (Belgrave) 13:35.

Report · page 46

Surrey CC League Division 2 Race 3, Lloyd Park, 8 Jan

Men's Road and Country

SURREY CC LEAGUE DIVISION 2 RACE 3 LLOYD PARK, 8 JAN

A fine win by Graham Adams, a welcome return to fitness and form by Jim Estall, and another solid performance by Phil Carstairs were the highlights of this third match in the Surrey league series. But in spite of finishing three in the first four and five in the first twelve, Belgrave, missing several of their middle order runners, were pipped for first place by home team Croydon. However, the Bels. retained the overall lead by a comfortable 294 points as they prepared for the final match at Wimbledon.

Lloyd Park always is tough and muddy but the incessant winter rain had made conditions about as bad as they could be. Older and wiser heads brought 18mm Javelin spikes for the occasion. This year the start was moved to a much improved position at the top of the playing fields and Belgrave captain Graham Adams soon showed at the front of the field as the runners tackled the first long hill.

Leading for the first mile, Gerry was then caught by Gary McIlroy of Epsom and Belgrave's Jim Estall, and the three fought tooth and nail, swapping the lead over the next three miles. Further back, Phil Carstairs was just off the pace, followed by Roger Alsop, perhaps a little wary before the Inter-Counties the following week. Half way through the final lap it looked as if McIlroy might be the stronger, but as the leaders left the strength sapping mud at the top of the course it was Adams who was able to change gear on the downward run in for home over the last half mile to win by 4 seconds from the Epsom man.

Jim Estall hung on for third (what an encouraging run after nearly a year of injury problems), ahead of Phil Carstairs who inched into the overall individual lead after three matches. Then Roger Alsop, a good 7th, and further back a steady run from Mark Anderson in 34th. Scoring for Belgrave for the first time Alex Luce, in 42nd, battled away very courageously. That made five for the Arthur Bruce squad out of the ten scorers (Colin Pearson please note)

However, Belgrave were missing several middle order runners and with Croydon packing well with their first ten runners finishing in 28, it was the home team who took the match by 21 points. Perhaps more worrying is the fact that in division one Belgrave would probably have scored well over 600 points, a position not unlike last year's. 1, G.Adams 30:22; 2, G.McIlroy (Epsom and Ewell) 30:26; 3, J.Estall 30:33; 4, P.Carstairs 30:49; 7, R.Alsop 31:18; 12, C.Dickinson (M45) 31:40; 22, Mike Lake 32:18; 30, R.Harding 32:58; 34, Mark Anderson 33:10; 43, A.Luce 33:50; 46, D.Anderson 33:56; 56, J.Galley 34:28; 77, J.Mather 35:33; 79, P.Sargent 35:41; 87, Marlon Anderson (J) 36:20; 92, A.Painter (M45) 36:41; 93, T.Williams 36:43; 108, J.Day 38:00; 111, M.Elliott 38:14; 115, S.Booth (J) 38:27; 129, D.Clack (M40) 40:54; 141, T.Lawton (M50) 43:32; 145, E.Derouet. Report · page 46

Belgrave 10 m CC Champ. Macher Shield, Wimbledon 22 Jan

A new two-lap course starting and ending at the top of Lauriston Road failed to tempt more Belgravians into taking part in the race for the Macher Shield; not surprising really when the course change only became apparent 30 minutes before the off! It actually turned out to be 10.2 miles for most of the field but who knows what distance the three leaders actually covered, for as they began the run towards home on the first lap they were somewhat confused to find that the rest of the field had unaccountably got ahead of them. The two Andersons and Leo Lyons completed the first circuit shoulder to shoulder and seemed to be discussing the route to be taken for the rest of the tour while two others had evidently had enough and headed for the showers leaving a mere six to set out once more. With his current state of fitness there was only going to be one winner and Leo Lyons duly obliged by going clear of Mark Anderson to earn the right to have his name placed on the shield.

Who will be the next winner? Well we may have some time to wait before we find out. With only six finishers "AW" correspondent Pete Mulholland was moved to point out: "This must be the first time you've had the whole result printed in Athletics Weekly," and those that run the Men's Road & Cross Country section have decided to put the race in limbo until such times as we have a reasonable number of club runners who again want to test themselves with 10 miles over the country. 1, L.Lyons 62:32; 2, Mark Anderson 62:59; 3, Marlon Anderson 65:18; 4, T.Williams 67:02; 5, D.McMillan 70:37; 6, J.Wasbrough 72:31. H'cap: 1, D.McMillan (23:30) 47:07; 2, L.Lyons (9:15) 53:17; 3, Mark Anderson (7:15) 55:44

Report · page 46

Surrey CC League Division 2 Race 4, Wimbledon, 12 Feb

Belgrave swept aside the opposition in the last Surrey League match on our home course at Wimbledon to win the match by over 200 points and the overall title by 500.

Led by stars Gary Staines and Kassa Tadesse in 1st and 2nd places, both happy to run in spite of the heavy mud, then came our engine room boys, Jim Estall, Graham Adams, Roger Alsop and Phil Carstairs, fighting it out for the overall individual award and all finishing in the first eleven. The scoring ten were completed by Matt Kinnane, Owain Lewes and Matt Hillier, who had a very fine first outing in the senior race.

With Alex Luce, Mike Lake, Mike Webb, Mark Anderson and Frank Ward all close behind, Belgrave would have made up for the loss at Croydon even without their stars. There was again a good team atmosphere and excellent turn-out to crown team manager Don Anderson's first year in charge.

The private battle between Adams, Alsop, Carstairs and Estall for the overall individual shield was won narrowly by the man in form, Jim Estall, after his excellent 5th place. Phil Carstairs never recovered from a poor start. He simply couldn't hear the starter and spent most of the race moving through the field nursing a pulled calf muscle. Roger Alsop came through well after a three week lay off but seemed to have his mind on road and track.

The only real disappointment was the presentation. After such a fine team effort it was a pity that so few went back to Belgrave Hall for the results and presentations. There was no championship trophy (it hadn't been returned from last year) and only one of the three individual winners was present. No mention was made of the runners who came such a close second and third. People felt that all their efforts over the four races had gone unnoticed. 1, G.Staines (Belgrave) 25:25; 2, K.Tadesse (Belgrave) 26:09; 3, M.Duncan (Woking) 26:50; ... 5, J.Estall 27:29; 6, G.Adams 27:38; 10, R.Alsop 27:59; 11, P.Carstairs 28:02; 16, M.Kinane 28:14; 23, O.Lewes 28:50; 25 C.Dickinson (M45) 28:53; 26, M.Hillier (U17) 28:54; 29, ... 29:12; 33, M.Webb 29:25; 36, M.Lake 29:33; 40, J.Galley 29:40; 42, F.Ward 29:44; 44, Mark Anderson 29:51; 46, S.Walker 30:02; 158 finished. Teams: 1, Belgrave 125; 2, Croydon 332; 3, Woking 337. Individuals: 1, J.Estall 29; 2, P.Carstairs 31; 3, R.Alsop 33. M40: 1, C.Dickinson 4.

Report · page 47

Belgrave '5¾' Champ., Wimbledon, 5 Mar

Graham Adams had it all his own way, cruising to a comfortable win while Charlie Dickinson, now well into his M45 age group, clocked a very pleasing best ever of exactly half an hour - pleasing, but what wouldn't he have given for a time just 1 second faster. A newcomer to the race was Hilary Saw, until now better known for holding a watch at the finish. Her run was remarkable for the fact that she had not a clue where the course went, ran the route entirely on her own and did not get lost!

Others who most certainly could not be classed as newcomers were Pat Newell and Charlie Walker. Our Hon. Handicapper Arthur Bruce recalled the day 45 years earlier when he and Chas first met. They battled their way up the "kiss of death" together with the latter chattering incessantly as the ascent was made. As the summit was reached his: "Are we going the right pace?" was finally met with Arthur's breathless, "I don't know who you are but for goodness sake shut up!" 1, G.Adams 29:10; 2, C.Dickinson 30:00; 3, O.Lewes 30:58; 4, J.Mather 32:21; 5, D.Anderson 33:24; 6, T.Williams 34:31; 7, P.Newell 36:41; 8, C.Walker 38:02; 9, D.Maughan 39:54; 10, H.Saw (W) 46:31. Report · page 47

South of England 12 Stage Road Relay, Thurrock, 3 Apr

The Belgrave team qualified for the National 12-Stage by finishing 13th in the Southern event which for the second year was held at Thurrock. Some might consider this the Belgrave 'B' team with only Darell Maynard surviving from the victorious team of four years ago and only Kassa Tadesse of our current stars performing. However, this was mainly the team that had supported the club so well in the winter Surrey League cross country races, many of whom train and race together, and it was fitting that they should represent the club in the relay and very pleasing that they ran well enough to qualify.

Thurrock really is a bleak venue for what at Wimbledon had been such an outstanding event. The long leg runners disappear somewhere towards the A13 and it is impossible to catch sight of them again until they return through the housing estate just before entering the stadium. The short leg was even shorter this year, only about 2¾ miles and dead flat. The only test was the very blustery wind.

Belgrave decided to kick off with their strongest runners - although it didn't look that way when Kassa, with one bandaged knee, ambled out of the stadium on the first leg about three places from the back of the field. Just over 20 minutes later he came back into the arena three from the front in a cracking time of 23:05 to hand over to Roger Alsop. Roger ran a 'blinder', the fastest of our short leg runners, pulling Belgrave through to 2nd with a time of 13:10. It looks as if Roger will really make inroads into his 5,000m pb this summer.

Jim Estall took over on leg three, keeping the team in touch with the leaders with another excellent leg. Then to Mark Anderson, lightly raced this winter and nursing a weak knee. Mark did well to hang on to the group in front until the very end and was pleased with his time of 13:45 (according to Colin Pearson's watch which was of course far more accurate than the official times!).

With the big stars from other teams appearing in the middle order, Belgrave slowly slipped down the field but with each member of the team running well there seemed every possibility of hanging on to a qualifying place in the first fifteen. Junior Galley, full of marathon training, ran over a minute faster than his time for Dulwich Runners last year, and he was followed by 800m man Rob Turner who floated round in 13:31. Over then to Phil Carstairs on the next long one, not at all confident of his form but good enough to run 25:19 - third fastest of our team.

On leg eight the wind which had been blustery all day turned to gale force. This and other factors such as age, wrong shoes, no lunch, etc., gave our runner (your correspondent) a time, corrected according to the George Stacey conversion table, at least one and a quarter minutes faster than the official one!

Leg nine saw our new Kiwi recruit Steve Walker, just back from a tour of Europe, running an excellent leg off long steady runs and no speed work. Alex Luce, fast changing from fun runner to real club athlete, returned a very respectable 14:16 and then Owain Lewes kept going at the back of the course, running himself almost to a standstill to get under 26:00.

By this time we were in 15th position as Owain handed over to Darrell Maynard for the last leg. Darrell likes to take the glory leg and it was a fine sight to see him outsprint two runners in the finishing straight to move the Bels up to 13th at the end. Teams: 1, Boxhill Racers 3:40:51; 2, Blackheath 3:42:50; 3, Boro' of Hounslow 3:43:26; ... 13, Belgrave 3:53:05; 50 teams finished.

Report · page 47

Hillingdon '5', 27 Feb

Mark Anderson

A traditionally large and strong field once again assembled for this major event on the road racing calendar, writes Mark Anderson. They all finished truly in the wake of an awesome Rob Denmark who literally ran away with the race in one of the fastest times seen over this undulating course.

A handful of claret and gold vests were prominent in the race, with Roger Alsop performing the best. He held on well after a 4:40 first downhill mile to finish in the top 30 with a similar time to last year's. Phil Carstairs admitted he felt rather dead during the race but he gutsed it out, however, over the last uphill mile to hold off Mark Anderson by 12 seconds. Alex Luce and Owain Lewes had a similar tussle with Alex managing to pull away towards the end. Alex has had a solid winter diet of cross-country racing and training with Arthur Bruce's squad and is now showing the benefits of his added strength. Laurie O'Hara completed the contingent with his usual solid run. His vast racing experience provided a welcome contrast to the youth and relative inexperience of the other Belgrave competitors. 30, R.Alsop 25:14; 57, P.Carstairs 26:18; 64, Mark Anderson 26:31; 74, A.Luce 27:07; 79, O.Lewes 27:14; 93, L.O'Hara (M60) 28:08.

Report · page 48

Diadora National 12 Stage Road Relay, Sutton Park, 30 Apr

As the majority of our team stretched their legs in the glorious sunshine after the minibus ride from Wimbledon to Birmingham there was a prevailing feeling of some nervousness. Not that we expected to win or, to be honest, get anywhere near the top 25% of the field. It's just that when you get outstanding runners like Darren and Kassa in the outfit, those engine-room boys who take over from such luminaries have to face the prospect of racing in exalted company and in these circumstances one can get swept along - only to die.

From the gun it was obvious that Kassa was in no mood for messin'. He stormed the hill just after the start in such a fashion that only three others could match him. London Irish's Burke hung on the longest and even attempted to come up to the Belgravian's shoulder as they swept past the pool towards the end of the lap. That was the last straw for the aggressive Kassa who later said, "I go fast - he come up to me - I go faster again!". Bels in the lead after lap one!

Roger Alsop seemed to enjoy the situation of setting out ahead of the cream of the land's road relay runners - perhaps it was the presence of the motorcycle borne cameramen filming his progress vs. Morpeth's international 1500m runner Terry West who was soon breathing down his neck. Roger held up well in spite of the pressure and was still in 2nd having run 10th fastest on his stage but with the favourites all close up behind it was inevitable that Phil Carstairs, in spite of running our second fastest long stage, found team after team slipping past him.

By the time a very fit looking Darren Mead took over to end the first half of the race we were in 29th. He was obviously in great shape having just returned from altitude training in Alberquerque but no-one was prepared for the staggering 13:49 that he unleashed. It might have been faster still but for the fact that he overdid it a bit early in the leg. By now the gaps had opened significantly, however, and even this fine run could only bring us up to 22nd. From there we settled into the second half of the field and at the end of the day, with team manager Donnie bringing us home in 34th before driving us back down the M40, we did not feel too dispirited. 1, Swansea 4:06:26; 2, Boxhill Racers 4:07:28; 3, Blackheath 4:07:47; ... 34, Belgrave 4:26:19; 54 teams finished.

Report · page 48

Jim Estall wins Club Marathon Champs

LONDON MARATHON, APRIL 17

JIM ESTALL retained the A.F.Mitchell Shield as first Belgrave runner with a fine 2:25:22 on a bitterly cold day. At the half-way mark there were no less than three Belgrave stars ahead of Jim and it looked as if we might even pull off the team prize.

Gary Staines was there to act as pace-maker for Eamon Martin and at Tower Bridge was leading a very large pack which included a slightly unhappy looking Paul Evans and about a minute further back was Kassa Tadesse. At this point Jim looked very controlled and comfortable. Then came a determined looking Leo Lyons followed closely by Junior Galley.

It wasn't long before Gary dropped out as planned, but Paul departed too, knowing that this was not his race, and at 19 miles Kassa felt his bad knee getting worse and also decided to call it a day.

The usual large band of Belgrave supporters at Southwark saw a wonderful race at the front end. At least the cold wind was with the runners as they came along the Embankment. Jim still looked good at this stage, concentrating hard, while Junior had passed Leo and was running strongly - his 2:29:44 was excellent for a 1500m man!

Leo has had an excellent season over the country and road but perhaps had a lack of mileage in his legs. At 23 miles it was a case of the mad staring eyes and it was guts and determination that got him to the finish as 3rd Belgravian in 2:44:44.

Then, very close together came Tony Verdie, 7th M55 and Richard Pearson. Both men are so experienced at running marathons and know how to get the most out of themselves whatever state of fitness they are in. Tony was delighted with his run and has lately stuck to a successful formula of the Autumn's Dublin Marathon and the Spring's London.

No doubt there were a few other Belgravians among the throng but the only ones we managed to spot in results shown in The Times during the next few days were:

70, J.Estall 2:25:22; 379, J.Galley 2:39:44; 621, L.Lyons 2:44:44; 1088, A.Verdie 2:52:02; 1103, R.Pearson 2:52:09; 2717, M.Graham 3:07:01; 4912, L.Mann 3:21:54; 13250, E.Derouet 4:01:21; 13654, D.Manning 4:03:26.

Report · page 49

Great Autumn Runs

Great Scottish Run Half Marathon August 21st, Glasgow. Although placing 4th in 1:03.56, just 2 seconds slower than his best ever, Kassa Tadesse was reportedly not particularly happy with his performance. Kamau of Kenya had run out the winner in 1:02:53 but the race for the minor places was only decided after a four man sprint towards the line. Wildbroad of Tanzania got one second clear of Rossendale's Dave Lewis for 2nd in 1:03:47 and Kassa was similarly 1 second ahead of McLoughlin of Liverpool.

Erewash Classic 10 miles September 4th Two Belgravians were among the 1100 strong field to contest what has been described as "the classiest 10 miler ever on British roads". Kassa Tadesse loves to stir up the pace and after two miles covered in 9:39 he burst from a pack that included Kenyans Kibor and Kamau, Willboard of Tanzania and many of the top Brits, to cover the third mile in 4:30. He eventually wound up 7th in 48:45 with Darren Mead 10th in 49:09. Another class runner and we would have "walked" the team race.

Half Marathon Record goes again

BUPA Great North Run September 18th, Newcastle Paul Evans' Club Record of 61:30, 4 seconds inside his previous best set last year, moved him clearly to number 1 in the UK rankings for '94 and allowed him to retain his British title. Superb running - but those Kenyans are something else again, sprinting for the line after almost exactly one hour's running and needing TV video replays to determine the actual winner. 1, B.Masya (Ken) 60:02; 2, M.Tanui (Ken) 60.03; 3, P.Tergat (Ken) 60:42; 4, P.Evans (Bel) 61:30.

Kassa outkicked

BUPA Great Caledonian Run 10k October 2nd, Edinburgh Gary Staines and one time World CC Champ Treacy were forcing the pace at 3k in 8:31, heading a bunch of seven that included Kassa and Rossendale's Dave Lewis. 5k was reached in 14:40 and at 6k, 17:32, Staines and Strizhakov of Russia were dropped when Treacy applied even more pressure. Kasse was still in contention with only 1000m remaining but it was Lewis who managed to dig deepest for victory. 1, D.Lewis (Rossendale) 28:56; 2, J.Treacy (Ireland) 28:58; 3, K.Tadesse (Belgrave) 29:05; ... 5, G.Staines 29:16.

A scorcher from Staines

BUPA Great South Run 10m October 9th, Portsmouth Gary Staines returned to the scene of his British Record breaking 10 mile run of 1993, took on some fine talent and ended up the winner again. Reportedly, he will be back again in '95 to go for a hat trick of wins. Kenya's Douglas Wakihuri turned up the heat at around 4 miles but they were back together again at halfway, reached in 23:22. Reaching 6 miles in 28:03 Gary held a slight advantage but his big effort came at 8 miles when he smoothly ran away from the Kenyan to earn a 23 second advantage by the finish on Southsea Esplanade. Third-placer Treacy of Ireland didn't get a look in this time. 1, G.Staines 47:00; 92, G.Adams 58:16.

"Rest of the World" just pip Bels.

BUPA Great Midland Run 10k October 16th, Coventry Kassa streaked away from the start line to make the first Belgrave showing but it was Gary Staines who crossed the finish line first with arms outstretched in pleasure at picking up another great win. Gary had sped through the first three kilos in 2:46, 5:39 and 8:36, setting the leading bunch going at a goodly pace until yet another Belgravian, Paul Evans, lobbed in a shocking 2:38 stretch from 5 to 6k that burst the field asunder. Only Staines and Dave Lewis could cope with that pace as the rest stretched out in their wake. At 8k Gary made an attempt to get away that proved indecisive but finished off by sprinting the last 400m to win by a single second. With Belgrave positions 1, 3 and 10 we can construct an unofficial team race v. the Rest of the World - Bels just three points in arrears of Lewis (England), Wakihuri (Kenya) and Treacy (Ireland). 1, G.Staines 28:40; 3, P.Evans 28:45; 10, K.Tadesse 29:24; 52, J.Gladwin 33:56.

Report · page 50

Belgrave 10k, Wimbledon, 23 Oct

You know, this is a race with a lot of potential. Starting on the Causeway, the accurately measured course consisted of one loop more or less following our Yacht '3' route, plus a longer lap down to Tibbets and then back across the Common on to the "Yacht" again to finish near the Village.

One hundred and eleven completed the race; fairly modest compared with other road races these days but contrast it with the bare ten or so that we now expect to turn out for the '5¾'. Times have changed and perhaps the days of ordinary Club Championships are numbered. Runners now expect large fields and even the 50-strong fields that we recall running in our road race champs. in the good old days would now be considered small beer - even if they were all Belgrave Harriers.

Nineteen of the field were women and 46 were not members of any athletic club - think of that potential membership.

As soon as the race began one could see that there was only going to be one winner. Blackheath's Simon Baines is no stranger to the Common having twice won the Varsity CC title there. He was never headed at any point and set a new best time for the event to take home the brand new shield awarded to the winner and donated by meeting organiser John Wasbrough. Marcello Bizio, gradually picking up fitness again, was first Belgravian - but only by three seconds from the ubiquitous Mike Webb. Our 'A' team was completed by Peter Lee, and these three were pipped for the team title by a Hercules Wimbledon outfit that was spear-headed by Steve Stockwell in 4th. "Come-backers" Mike Manning and Tony Binda were among several other Bels. to run well.

Good work "Wassie", in putting on this event - and thanks also go to Hercules Wimbledon's Pete Mulholland for producing the results. 1, S.Baines (Blackheath) 29:58 (record); 2, E.Hurley (Fleet &C) 31:26; 3, B.Attwell (Shaftesbury) 32:28; .... 6, M.Bizio 33:44; 7, M.Webb 33:47; 14, P.Lee 37:33; 15, M.Manning 38:08; 16, R.Mann 38:16; 19, C.Cooper 38:27; 25, P.Newell 39:40; 34, L.Mann 40:51; 43, A.Kassim 42:18; 47, R.Bale 42:50; 53, C.Walker 43:25; 63, E.Derouet 45:09; 111 finished. Teams: 1, Hercules Wimb. 23; 2, Belgrave 'A' 27; 3, Belgrave 'B' 49; 4, Belgrave 'C' 78. Women: 1, L.Caplan (Unattached) 43:00; 2, J.Daley (Proactive) 43:36; 3, B.Andrews (Chesham) 48:07.

Feature · page 50

Evans and Staines in bid for "World Cross" ?

Not recently having shown a great liking for the country, Paul Evans, at the age of 33, has started to exhibit the sort of confidence that he has always had in abundance when racing on the road. He turned down a spot in the European Championship race at Alnwick but grabbed 6th place in Algiers over 9,450 metres (27:07.7) in the IAAF World Cross Challenge on 4th December. Two weeks later in Brussels he was 6th again in the same series, this time covering the 10.5k in 32:07. But it wasn't so much his final position that caught the eye, even if he was first Briton home, it was the way he got there. He was way down the field in 47th spot after the first of the seven 1.5k laps but then swept to 15th on the second circuit, 10th on the third and 7th by the end of lap four. So pleased was he with his performance that afterwards he was stated to be looking to make England's World Championships team in March before switching back to the roads again.

Gary Staines has also been running into good winter form following his super series of Autumn runs on the road and both turned out to compete in the Durham International Cross Country Race on the last day of 1994. The course took in 9.2 kilometres of 'proper' cross country and included a hill that had some of the back-markers resting hands on knees as they endeavoured to keep going up its severe slope on their fifth and sixth ascents. Both Paul and Gary raced exceptionally well, showing great strength as the race progressed to place 4th, 30:20, and 6th, 30:42. The course, with minor alterations is to be used for the World Championships and what a superb prospect it would be to see both of them racing for the UK in the premier cross country event.

Paul EvansGary Staines

Feature · page 50

Belgrave road runners rated 4th in UK

Examination of the national points table at the end of the Star Rank Road Running Series brought up some interesting facts. The table was headed by miles by Bridgend's Steve "Brace the Race" who competed in no less than eleven of the designated events to score a massive 1,290 points. The next most prolific racer was Clark of Poole who turned out eight times but only scored 790 points for 3rd while Omega's Udall gained 834 from four events for 2nd.

Belgrave were very well represented: Kassa Tadesse ranked 8th with five races and 607 points, Darren Mead 24th from four races with 393, and Gary Staines 49th from two races with 246. Only Bridgend and Westbury had four runners in the top fifty with the Bels joining Swansea and Omega on three.

Total points earned by clubs would seem to indicate that based on this competition Belgrave are rated number four road running club in the country!

Report · page 51

Yacht Handicap, Wimbledon, 1 Oct

It's worth being up at the Hall before the 'Yacht' just to hear the insults flying about. Who'd be the handicapper when Dave McMillan's in the race. Charlie Manning's assertion that Dave must be Hon. Handicapper 'Boofer' Bruce's illegitimate son and that he might as well be presented with first prize before the start, brought forth the retort: "Look it's no use you moaning Chas. - I give you an extra minute every year - in a few year's time you're going to have to set out on Friday night!"

In actual fact Dave literally is getting faster as he gets older. Regular training has lightened his 47 year old frame considerably and this autumn he set a pb in placing 30th in the Maidenhead Half-Marathon. His 17:18 around the Common equalled his best ever - although his agent John Martin says that he should have been credited with a 17:13 a few years back when there was an error in the arithmetic. If Mike Farrelly hadn't glanced nervously over his shoulder in the last stretch and seen him coming, Dave would have pulled it off yet again.

It was good to see Tony Binda racing again after an absence of too many years, while for new member Jesper Madsen it was his first time in competition with us. Fastest of the day was Phil Carstairs, a fact that didn't go down too well with Mr. Alsop who didn't seem to think it at all possible.

Report · page 51

Surrey CC League Div. 1 Match 3, Lloyd Park, 7 Jan

Lying in 7th spot in the league with two clubs behind us we really needed a good effort to draw us clear of the relegation zone and with two of our 'aces' among the 170-odd starters we were hopeful of a 5th or 6th team place. It didn't work out that way.

Kassa headed the field at the first rise with Aldershot's Jackson in close attendance, but a pack of serious contenders were not far behind and keeping an eye on the leaders. Whereas Kassa was in command, however, the Aldershot man had already overcooked things and by the end of the first circuit it was a cool and calm looking Gary Staines who had joined Kassa along with Harris of Boxhill. Jim Estall was well forward but it was a long and ominous wait until our next scorers came by in the late 60s.

Heading out into the country again Matt Hillier was our fourth scorer ahead of Graham Adams. Matt fell on a slippery descent, but no sympathy came from our Captain who pointedly told him to, "get up and get on with it!"

Out on the back of the course Gary put the boot in and broke clear of his rivals, allowing himself the luxury of a gentle run in to the finish. Kassa saw that he had shut the power off and sprinted hard as the funnel was approached but Gary had it all under control, smiling as he kept one second between them.

Belgrave one and two! Jim Estall was soon in at 18th, just one place down on fellow veteran Dave Hill, but then it was a long, long wait for our fourth man. Belgrave supporters' brains and fingers were doing overtime as they struggled with the maths but it was clear by the time that our ten were in that we had done no better than in the previous two races. We do seem to be just about keeping our heads above water in Division One but if Gary and Kassa hadn't turned out we would have been last on the day - and LAST IN THE LEAGUE! Engine-room? With no disrespect to anyone who took part (the scorers included three veterans and two first year juniors) - what engine-room? 1, G.Staines 27:48; 2, K.Tadesse 27:49; 3, S.Harris (Boxhill) 27:49; 18, J.Estall 29:15; 62, G.Adams 31:01; 64, M.Hillier 31:05; 81, M.Webb 31:34; 97, P.Carstairs 32:15; 111, A.Jones 32:54; 115, M.Nouch 33:04; 129, J.Mather 33:55; 151, W.Swaffer 36:01; 170, T.Earle 43:19; 170 finished. Report · page 51

Surrey CC League Div. 1 Match 2, Brockwell Park, 19 Nov

This was a better performance by the Belgrave team. With several middle order runners beginning to find their fitness after the Autumn lay off it only needed an appearance from one or two of our top runners for Belgrave to have finished with quite a good score. As it was, Belgrave came 7th on the day ahead of Croydon and Hercules.

Again Matt Kinane led the team home in 20th position after a very good run. Then came Graham Adams, Jim Estall, Roger Alsop, Phil Carstairs and Matt Hillier all fairly close and swapping positions throughout the race. Out of these it was Captain Graham who just finished the strongest. They are now really the backbone of our side and can all improve to inside the top thirty when fully fit.

Then a gap before Said Chettati and Junior Galley who had a fine battle, stirring memories of their races over 3000m in the Rosenheim League last summer.

Adam Jones suffered from starting too fast, not wise on this very tough undulating course, but he will learn and improve. Then good old Mike Lake, up from Hampshire for the day, completed the scoring in 112th.

Further back, Mike Nouch, now a veteran and gradually regaining his fitnes, was in 129th, and Trevor Williams was only just behind Don Anderson in 156th.

Report · page 52

Women's Surrey CC League Division 1 Race 4, Reigate Priory, 19 Feb

And so to the playing fields and massive climb that make up the Reigate course. A good turn-out by Thames Hare & Hounds could have got them back to win the series and we were without Gabby Collison so the scene was set for a nail-biting finish. Once the field set out, however, it was obvious that it was Woking AC that were at full strength and looked likely to be comfortable team winners.

At the half-way stage, running through the woods at the foot of the hill, it was Morag Andrew leading our contingent but close behind were Helen Maskrey and Martina Ryan and a little further back again was Nikki Haines making her debut in Belgrave colours. By the finish Helen had eased past Morag to lead the Bels. in with 5th, 6th, 8th and 18th places (Nikki full of apologies for only being 18th!). Woking had indeed clinched the team spot by occupying 2nd, 3rd and 4th before getting their last scorer home just in front of ours.

Now the marathon part of the proceedings began as the final scores were worked out for all the age-groups in both divisions with the entire burden seeming to fall on the shoulders of the league secretary Ron Gobey of Herne Hill. Eventually Belgrave were declared overall winners of division one with match positions of 2nd, 2nd, 1st and 2nd - a historic victory. But the awards didn't stop there. Gabby was declared outright individual winner (three best performances out of the four score in this) - and Helen was second. 1, A.Fletcher (Dulwich R) 17:29; 2, L.Taylor (Woking) 17:46; 3, R.Fay (Woking) 17:48; ... 5, H.Maskrey 17:51; 6, M.Andrew 17:54; 8, M.Ryan 18:13; 18, N.Haines 18:46 ... Report · page 52

Gabby 2nd in opening race

SURREY WOMEN'S CC LEAGUE, DIVISION 1 RACE 1 PUTNEY VALE, 22 OCT

This had to be one of the toughest courses ever seen on Wimbledon Common. It started and finished innocently enough on the playing fields near Beverley Brook but with the rain falling in stair-rods and causing the most slippery of conditions, the circuit took in long stretches of horse-ride and the muddy, near vertical climbs in the woods on the far side of Queensmere Pond.

West 4's Sutherland (on loan from Serpentine Runners who don't have a Surrey Cross Country team, and running in the Division Two match held in conjunction) made the running immediately the gun was fired. Gabby was in close attendance and as the race progressed became the only real challenger with even Croydon's Jeina Mitchell finding the pace too hot. But the West 4 runner just seemed to get stronger and stronger until, at the climb on the last lap, it became a damage limitation exercise for the Belgrave girl who had to be content with 2nd.

Good forward running came from from Nikki Haines and Jacinta Moore, and modern pentathlete Kate Houston surprised a few with her 37th but with a few absences through injury and maternity, it was 5th place overall for the Bels.

Feature · page 53

Clear Centenary Cup win for Gabby Collison

The award of the Centenary Cup was a pretty straightforward affair for 1994. Gabby Collison was outstanding during the summer, carrying over her new level of performance from the cross-country season onto the track and road. She topped our rankings at everything from 800 to 5,000 metres plus 5 miles, 10k and half-marathon on the road. Her runs in the Reebok Track Challenge 5,000m at Crawley (16:44.58) and Southern Inter-Counties 3,000m at Sutcliffe Park where she was 2nd (9:39.8) both constituted Club Records. The 5k was a full 50 seconds faster than Rosemary Honeychurch's mark from 1992 while New Zealander Wendy Cotterell lost her 3k Belgrave best by 9 seconds. At 1,500m she fell just 1/10th short of Hilary Baxter's 14 year old Belgrave best of 4:31.4. Gabby's 5 mile race at Victoria Park back in April places her 5th in the country for 1994 and while the situation for Club Bests for Women on the Road is not clear at the moment (we need some statistical work in this area) one thing is for sure - all of her road marks must be in contention for record status. The Belgrave Committee had no trouble at all in agreeing that the Centenary Cup should be awarded to Gabrielle for her outstanding Club Record of 16:44.58 for the 5,000 metres.

Gabby CollisonRosemary HoneychurchWendy CotterellHilary Baxter

Notice · page 53

Officials will score in Women's SL matches

New moves have been made in the Women's Southern T&F League to attempt to ensure that clubs bring along their correct quota of officials. Each Club is supposed to provide three field officials, one track judge and a timekeeper - and almost invariably we do. Many clubs do not, however and in 1995, for the first time, five points will be awarded at each match for each official supplied. These will go towards the clubs' scores on the day. Five officials means an extra 25 points and as an added bonus each club can take along an extra official to gain an additional five points. It will be interesting to see what effect this new rule has.

See it on the printed page →
Report · page 53

Sports injuries lecture at Belgrave Hall

Back in September we welcomed Debbie Annett, Sports Therapist, along to Belgrave Hall where she and our own Tony Ganio, talked enthusiastically about sports injuries - how to avoid them and what action to take when they arise. The talk was mainly aimed at Team Managers and Coaches so that they should at least know the basic treatment required when their athletes run into problems. The main failing we all have, it seems, is not getting expert advice when problems first arise. It's so much easier to ignore it and run on for a few weeks to see if it goes away!

Also on hand was Mike Bazire demonstrating mobility exercises. Mike often runs the mobility sections of the South of England Coaching Sessions - he's well known for his flexibility. Half an hour of Mike's gentle torture had the writer stiff as a board for the next few days but frankly, it's the part of training that most runners completely disregard. Correct stretching before violent activity goes a long way to ensuring that those injuries don't crop up in the first place.

It was good to pick up on the expert advice from these people and we must make sure that the evening is repeated.

In the meantime, should you require any sports injury treatment don't hesitate to get in touch with Debbie at the following address. She will give 20% discount if you show proof that you are a Belgrave club member.

SPORTS INJURY THERAPY SERVICES OWEN HARRIS & DEBBIE ANNETT "The Surgery", Brunswick Lodge 199A Ewell Road, Surbiton, Surrey KT6 6BB Tel: 0181-399 4627 Sunday Emergency Tel: 0181-337 6014

Debbie AnnettTony GanioMike BazireOwen Harris

SURREY CC LEAGUE,DIV. 1 MATCH 3, LLOYD PARK, 7 JAN Bels lead both age-groups in Surrey League

Simon is running into form nicely with the big championships just around the corner - and this with another year in the age group. He was just clear of Gavin and Tom Evans who fought a desperate struggle to the very last.

Simon is lying 3rd in the individual competition with Nigel Whorlow 2nd of the under 15s.

Another example of the Belgrave lads fighting to be the first in for their club came when the under 13s started streaming in. Christian Morris was ahead of Robert Bignall by a whisker and stayed that way, both being given the same time. Their battle carried them to 1st and 2nd positions in the race and they currently lie 3rd and 5th in the individual stakes after three races. Feature · page 55

A full year at Shepway

Steve Whorlow

We have an unwritten agreement in Shepway and that being - "We will all work together to support each other". It works. There are no best or worst, rich or poor, friend or foe, just "Club-mates". We may not always agree with each other, or even be friends all the time, but we do support each other during training and competition.

So how is it done? Well, the longer standing athletes help with newcomers and older athletes help coach the younger ones. Every athlete knows that they are needed in one form or another. This way the group grows, all learning from each another. There is no jealousy, just admiration of those that have achieved recognition.

During the first year the group grew to about 35, with 25 of them being active. Now there are 60 (15 girls and 45 boys/youths) of which 48 have represented the Club during the track season. Fortytwo have renewed their membership while others have either taken up other activities or just lost interest in athletics. This gives us an active membership in the group of 69%, which when measured against other youth activities is a good ratio.

Adult membership has also grown. We now have five seniors helping with coaching and two wives who have joined as family members. I look after shot, discus and middle distance, and then we have Ron Permaul (sprinting). Sharron Dray (hurdles and sprinting), Liz Smythe (javelin and long jump), and Steve (long jump and triple jump), with all of us filling in as required.

As mentioned earlier the older athletes also help with the training sessions. This has led to Wesley Swaffer, Bobby Peacock, Chris Wilson, Nathan Scamp and Daniel Newman wanting to undertake coaching courses during the winter

Returning to the subject of membership, each new member is introduced to the club through the other members or their activities. Up until now no adult has asked a potential club member to join. This must be to the credit of each and every member of the group in Shepway, that their succcesses and activities alone is the inspiration for others.

The only exception to this has been the "Startrack" scheme run by the B.A.F. in August. The group was asked by the local authority to run the event on their behalf. It was a great success and has produced some U13 boys and girls for the club's coming winter and summer seasons. As a result of this scheme we now hope to start a U11 group with the intention of having a feeder group for the U13's. If we can encourage this younger group to grow then it can only have a good result in the years to come.

During the past-fifteen months we have had our successes although we all realise that it will take a couple of years to produce athletes of the highest quality. But we have had some cases where athletes have been called upon to compete in the higher leagues because others have not been available. Being available at the right time is one of the ways to the top.

Wesley Swaffer competed in the British League and also in the Southern League along with Bobby Peacock. There have been medals in the Kent county championships - Nigel Whorlow, 3000m walk (gold), Wesley Swaffer, 2000m steeplechase (bronze), Amy Butler, 2500m walk (bronze), Jason Stevens, 600m (bronze) as well as two finalists, Paul Hills (100m and 200m - 4th in both) and Bobby Peacock (400m - 5th). A county vest was won by Nigel Whorlow for the U15 Inter-Counties 2000m walk (3rd) and a week later he competed as an U17 in the Inter-Counties at Enfield (2nd). This was followed by his selection for the Kent County Schools team at the English Schools Walking championship. (Not bad for someone who took up walking just to pass the time.)

Moses Semakula took the Thameside League Boys' best performance 1994, as well as gaining three Grade I standards as an U13.

In the last year's cross country season Wesley Swaffer took the individual title in Surrey League with Bobby Peacock runner up, and Nigel Whorlow was runner up in the U13 group. We also won the team event at the Tonbridge Open (U13Boys) in March.

On the road the group won the team event in the Hastings Half Marathon Mini-run (2km) - Wesley 6th, Nathan 11th, Nigel 13th and Bobby 14th. There were 208 finishers in the event which puts the boys' successes into perspective.

Although the group's success is only relative to the competition available at the time, and in the N.Y.A.L we only managed a scattering of wins and placings, we are sure that this first full year of competition has been a process of learning. Hopefully we can all improve for the coming year.

One thing that has been enjoyed by the whole group is the travel to the many places. When you remember that we are 75 miles from Battersea, each and every trip is an adventure for the younger athletes. We have noted how far we have been during the last year and to our surprise it has totalled over 7000 miles. We only missed 2 matches out of 35 cross country, track and field.

Finally, it's not only the youngsters that have been competing. Sharon Dray and Liz Smythe have competed in club events as well as Stephen Whorlow Snr and Ron Permaul, just to show the youngsters that there's life in the oldies yet.

Many thanks from all in Shepway for making it possible for us to compete in so many events and at a level that suits all ages and abilities. We will continue to give the club our total support for many years to come.

Steve Whorlow

Steve WhorlowRon PermaulSharron DrayLiz SmytheSteveWesley SwafferBobby PeacockChris WilsonNathan ScampDaniel NewmanNigel WhorlowAmy ButlerJason StevensPaul HillsMoses SemakulaStephen Whorlow Snr
See it on the printed page →
Report · page 56

BVAF Open National CC Champs., Tunbridge Wells, 27 Mar

Veterans' Results

Road & Country

BVAF OPEN NATIONAL CC CHAMPS. TUNBRIDGE WELLS, 27 MAR

An outstanding win by Laurie O'Hara and a team gold medal by the Belgrave M60 boys were the highlights of these championships, so well organised by David Coward of VAC in the playing fields next to his school in Tunbridge Wells.

This year no long haul up to Burnley or Cardiff so a fairly relaxed start from Belgrave Hall with a scattering of runners from the different age groups plus Arthur Bruce and Fred Paget but disappointingly only one team.

Although very late in the cross country season, the winter rain had continued right up to the last to produce a very tough, saturated grassy course with some short undulations on a long haul up to the finish on each of the three laps, and thick mud in one or two places. A strong wind made conditions over the six and a half miles very testing.

The ladies were the first to run and unfortunately our new star Jacinta Moore had to withdraw after being up all night with her baby. So Sue Porter decided not to come (she had gamely agreed to make up the scoring three) and this left Nina Mills as Belgrave's sole runner. Nina ran well to finish 42nd, 11th in the W45 category.

Next came the combined M50 and M60 race. Laurie ran a blinder. He finished an amazing 31st in the actual race, easily first in the M60 event and over 80 seconds ahead of Maurice Morrell who had gone past a tired looking Ron Higgs in the latter stages of the race. Although he looked rough on the first lap, Pat Newell came through strongly to finish 10th, finding the course much more to his liking than the long hills of Burnley last year. Pat was less than half a minute behind the in form M65 winner Steve Charlton. Next was Charlie Walker, running a great deal better than he himself believed. Charlie did more than enough to keep ahead of Barnet's Laurie Forster. Bert Stroud is not quite as fit as last year but did well to finish 15th although he did look very tired on the last lap.

Neither Tony Stone or Tony Hardy are fully fit after long injury problems but both completed the course; Tony Hardy in 78th and Tony Stone 126th. The latter was also helping with the results.

The M40 to M49 race saw an excellent victory for the new supervet Nigel Gates of Brighton & Hove. Belgrave had just two competitors, both in the M45 section. Charlie Dickinson looked to be getting close to the bronze half way through the race after overtaking Cardiff winner Harry Mathews, but finally had to settle for 5th. Leo Lyons was a little disappointed with his run after such a good season in the VAC races. Perhaps he hadn't fully recovered from his half marathon the week before. But good to see Leo competing in a National for the first time. M45 1, A.Roper (Swansea) 34:08; 2, B.O'Neil (Aldershot & F) 34:36; 3, P.Probin (Imperial) 35:17; ... 5, C.Dickinson 35:28; 26, L.Lyons 37:55. Report · page 56

BAF & BVAF National Road Relay Championships, Rushmoor Arena, 21 May

A magnificent run by Laurie O'Hara swept the Belgrave M60 stars into the gold medal position on the final leg and made the journey to Aldershot on a cold, wet and windy day worthwhile. At last we had beaten Barnet in the relay! A rather unfit M50 team completed the race too, and did well to finish 21st. The usual last week drop outs meant that we only had threequarters of an M40 team which was a pity because we were lying 38th, our best ever position, after 6 legs.

So in all it was a good day for Belgrave in spite of the appalling conditions, lack of facilities and the sense of occasion generated when the relay is held at Sutton Park. It was a pity that there were noticably fewer teams competing, particularly from the North.

In the first race the M50's, M60's and women set off together. For Belgrave, Tony Verdie, fresh from a very good Tadworth '5' and seen lately doing speedwork on the track at Battersea, sat confidently at the rear of the leading pack as the runners came up the first hill. Further back was Pat Newell with his eyes on J.D. Wood of Barnet. By the end of the first leg Tony had moved through to 12th with Pat a steady 4th in the M60's behind Spink of Bingley, Abel of Stonemasters, and only 13 seconds behind Wood of Barnet.

Mike Newton showed that all his natural speed had not disappeared with his lack of fitness and returned an excellent 24:19 on leg two of the 50's. Charlie Walker, perhaps still not at his best after recent injury, nevertheless kept Belgrave well in touch in the 60's losing another 44 seconds to Barnet's Laurie Forster, but more importantly keeping the gap to less than a minute. At this stage the leaders were still Bingley - just.

A year ago at Sutton Park it was the excellent first leg by Barnet's Ron Higgs, only 8 seconds slower than Laurie, that lost Belgrave the race. This year it was a different story. Ron isn't running as well and Laurie is running even better. In fact, even though we have never seen him run badly, surely Laurie has never shown so much determination and aggression. Both runners soon overtook the Bingley man and the Barnet lead of 57 seconds had been reduced to less than 25 after the first lap. All eyes looked to the far side of the field as the leaders came into sight at the top of the final hill. Laurie swept down the incline past poor Ron Higgs and then sprinted the final half mile to win by no less than 27 seconds. It was an outstanding performance and Laurie very nearly caught Alan Black running third in the 50's.

Belgrave M50s remained in the low 20's for the final three legs run by Bill Laws, John Wasbrough and Carl Cross. It was a solid performance with so many of the team not as fit as they would like.

It was disappointing again not to have a full M40's team. Such a good start too with Charlie Dickinson 4th on the first leg in 19:36 handing over to the man in form, Leo Lyons, who had a great battle all the way round with the Bromsgrove runner before forging into the lead up the final hill. Then of course it was a gradual drop but a solid team of Richard Pearson, John Mather (running in his first veteran event), Rex Bale and Tony Stone were in 38th place at the end of the sixth stage. At the front there was a magnificent race with Mike Hager and John Wheway both running well under 19 minutes and moving Tipton up from 7th to 1st on the final two legs. Report · page 56

BVAF National 5000m Road Championships, Dunsfold Airfield, 24 Apr

The National Veterans' 5000m Road Championships returned to Dunsfold Airfield after an absence of two years - a return to the usual windy conditions, this time "against" all the way down the far side of the course. Although very well organised by our friends from VAC, this event has never been over popular with Belgrave runners.

In fact it was only the M60 team which ran on the day, just losing out by one point to close rivals Barnet. Laurie O'Hara took the individual M60 title by over a minute in a new record time of 17:41. Until about 900 metres to go it looked as if the M60 team race was in the bag. Laurie was well out in front, Pat was running strongly ahead of Laurie Forster of Barnet, and Charlie Walker, with his bad leg holding out well, was just behind Derek Wood. But then it all changed. Pat and Charlie went back, Forster and Wood came through and with Ron Higgs 3rd it ended up with Barnet shading Belgrave by a point. M60 1, L.O'Hara 17:41; 2. J.Portsmore (Fleet & Crk) 18:44; 3. Feature · page 57

A good year for "gutsy " Leo

Leo Lyons has been running for Belgrave for many years and has always had a reputation for training and racing hard. A very determined and "gutsy" distance runner, he has had more than his fair share of injuries which have often interrupted the flow of good performances; the usual leg injuries have been most common but Leo has also suffered from sciatica and two years ago cracked his shinbone on a steeplechase barrier.

Now just turned 45 and at last free from major injury, Leo has been enjoying his best and most consistant form as a veteran with fine wins over the country in VAC events last winter, good times at the half and full marathon and lately, his best ever veteran performances on the track.

In fact he won no less than four of those popular VAC cross country races, with a best time of 26:32, and was only beaten in the fifth by Brighton's Nigel Gates. Leo also collected last season's Belgrave 10 mile cross country shield and ran in the BVAF championships at Tunbridge Wells in March.

On the road he was 5th overall and first vet. in the Swanley half-marathon with 73:04. He took the VAC title and beat Keith Penny into the bargain. His 2:44:44 in the London Marathon gave him the bronze medal in the Belgrave Championship and a cracking second leg run in the National Veterans' Road Relay at Aldershot brought the Bels. a very exciting and rare (although brief) lead.

Under 4:30 for 1500m and just over 16 minutes for 5000m on the track, it was in the steeplechase that he had his most notable performances of the 1994 season. Not the smoothest of hurdlers, Leo relies on his strength to get him through this the toughest of all events. With a good win behind him in the Vets' T&F League, he romped home in the SCVAC Champs at Swindon in 10:16 to set a new record by over a minute. His main target for the 1994 track season, however, was the BVAF 3k 'chase at Bedford in July, and it's good to be able to report that he achieved what he set out to do by winning the M45 race in 10:31.25.

Well done Leo. Good luck - and keep injury free.

Leo LyonsNigel GatesKeith Penny

Report · page 58

SCVAC T&F League Mid-London Div., Match 1, Battersea Park 25 Apr

Track & Field

SCVAC T&F LEAGUE MID-LONDON DIV., MATCH 1 BATTERSEA PARK 25 APR

Last year we thought we were going to do well but then blew it with a couple of very poor turn-outs. We now felt that our men's and women's were stronger all-round, so what would the 1994 season bring us?

From the very beginning of the meeting bad luck hit us with a "double-whammy" when Bob Bridges pulled a calf muscle simply limbering up for the 100 metres - and then John Jeffery, really looking forward to the 400, "popped" a ham string when nearing the tape in his over 50's 100 dash. Tough luck on both of them and we could only hope they would both recover before the season was over.

Serpentine Runners, with a competitor in virtually every event, were run away winners in the women's match. But with new team members Marcia Harewood, Maureen Jones and Jacinta Moore to bolster us, we surprised the Met. Police by pushing them down to 3rd. Jacinta was devastating, clearly winning the 400 and then within 25 minutes doing another demolition job on the 1500 field. She picked up 32 points for that double and then chipped in with a relay leg. Barbara Dunsford, our sole over 50 representative, was another 32 pointer and with Sue Porter leading the walkers home we were pleased with our start to the season.

It was very tight indeed in the men's match. Mike Small enjoyed his very first veteran competition and would have been our highest scorer but for the fact that he took a non-scoring role in the shot, allowing new member Pan Zeniou, an international level decathlete from a few years back, to get some points on the board. Messrs. Warburton, King and Fotheringham gave us a nigh on maximum score in the walk - what a pity there are no more walks until match three - but not surprisingly, our top male scorer was Captain "Snowy" Brooks, in the thick of the action on track and field. We had great support from the distance men (we fell down there last season) and it was a delight to see Charlie Dickinson take off in the 400 like it was a 10k, passing 200 metres in last position and then all but reeling in the entire field along the home stretch. Report · page 58

SCVAC T&F League Mid-London Div., Match 2, Battersea Park 16 May

"Yep! Sometimes it's good being an old lady," chuckled Barbara Dunsford, nudging our team manager in the ribs after his congratulations to her on three more wins. It was another enjoyable evening for the Old Bels. although the men's team, looking for a win, were surprised to find themselves only third with Serpentine and Thames Valley getting the jump on them.

Leo Lyons is running better than ever. The winter has seen him tearing away from the field at the start of every veterans' race in which he has taken part, laying down a challenge to all participants. It's a habit he's carried over onto the track and, running in flat shoes, he left the rest of the steeplechasers in his wake while removing a further half a minute off his M45 Club Record. What a treat it was, too, to see tough-guy Pete Gilbey back in action. A great technician over the barriers, Pete took the 'B' race by a street and was good enough to win this and probably any other SCVAC 'chase, but for clashing with an in-form Leo. He must have been pleased with himself too, because news of his run soon spread to the East Street Gym in Epsom where Pete does a lot of his workouts.

Natalie Stracey and Nina Mills made their '94 debuts in individual events and both were involved in the final race, a medley relay that had competitors and officials from all clubs jumping with excitement. Pat Mead ran her socks off on the second 200 stint to keep us in contention with the "Serps" but they had a strong 400 runner who got away from Nina, still blowing hard after her 3k race. Now it was all up to Jacinta on the final 800 metre leg. Imperceptibly she closed over the first lap but was still 50 metres down at the bell. Remember, she had already won the 800 and 3,000. Surely this was just too much. But she continued to close ... quicker ... and quicker ... until, to the delight of all those present who were not wearing red vests with two yellow hoops, she tore past and into the lead.

In match one our women ended up 115 points down on Serpentine Runners but here the gap was down to 51. Our women were on their way - and we had a secret weapon up our sleeves for match three! Report · page 58

SCVAC T&F Championships, Swindon, 29 May

Only three Belgravians made the long journey to Swindon for this year's SCVAC Championships but Snowy Brooks, Leo Lyons and Charlie Dickinson all came away with a gold medal apiece, with Snowy collecting four second places as well.

Although the rain mostly held off, it was a blustery day on a very exposed track next to the new Swindon Town football stadium. Although not too well supported in numbers there were many outstanding performances and championship bests, including a 10.16.7 run by Leo Lyons in the M45 Steeplechase where he sliced over a minute off the previous best.

Elsewhere we were treated to a 54.78m hammer throw by BBC commentator Paul Dickenson, a 4.30m pole vault by Brian Hooper, and an 11.6/23.5 sprint double by Alastair Ross.

Snowy won the M45 long jump with a leap of 5.31m, came second in the 100m and 200m in 13.0 and 27.1, and also gained 2nds in the discus and shot with throws of 33.60m and 10.34m. Charlie Dickinson was beaten in the 5000m by new vet Bill Bailey, an army man from Aldershot, but easily won the M45 category in 15:46.7.

Snowy BrooksLeo LyonsCharlie DickinsonPaul DickensonBrian HooperAlastair RossBill Bailey

Report · page 58

SCVAC T&F League Mid-London Div., Match 3, Hendon, 27 Jun

Our women's team had their best competition yet, beginning to look a sure bet to be our first ever Vets team to reach the SCVAC final as they consolidated 2nd position overall. With two ladies now operating in the W50 group we scored heavier than ever before, our points differential with the Serps now down to a mere 22 in this match. Barbara Dunsford rushed back from Bruges to set four pbs and was joined for the first time by our secret weapon, Cynthia James fresh into the age group. Another best ever came from Sally Scott, her 15.1 getting her frighteningly within range of a slot in Southern League relay teams.

It was not such a happy story in the men's match. Work commitments kept a couple of our key competitors away and Leo Lyons ran with back trouble, making all the difference between 1st team place and 3rd in a tightly scored match. Bob Bridges and Snowy Brooks were able to put their rivalry to the test in the sprints and ended the evening being just tenths apart when the times were added up. Bob also broke new ground in the hammer - and what do you think of young Georgie Piddington - all the way up from Hayling Island on the South Coast for an evening filling in as reserve! Report · page 59

SCVAC T&F League Mid-London Div., Match 4, Tooting, 25 Jul

And so to the last matches in our division. The men had to win by miles to get through to the final but all our women needed to do was to turn up on the night. As it happened, both teams won handsomely for our first ever victories but as far as the men were concerned there just weren't quite enough points in the bag.

A SCVAC T&F League Record for M50s came from Mike Edwards in the pole vault with a fine 3.20. Leo Lyons was back in form and good points were scored in the throws by Mike Small and Pani Zeniou. Paul Warburton did the business for us in the 2k walk, backed up well by Lionel Mann and Johnny Morris. Once again, however, poor Bob Bridges had to resort to the Koolpak - a pulled hamstring in the 100m!

As for the women - they were great! We had just about every event covered for the first time, even the over 50's 5k where Pam Davies must have found it difficult to believe that she was back competing on Tooting track where her athletic career started in the 1950s. Sue Porter, Heather Miller and Margaret Stroud gave us a treble in the walk and Cynthia James mopped up points all over the arena but the hardest won victories came from Jacinta Moore.

Jacinta took the 5,000 metres and then lined up for the 800, the very next event, and not surprisingly had to dig down exceedingly deep to stay ahead down the home straight - but it wasn't over yet. We needed to win the relay to clinch the match and that 4x 200 metres turned out to be so exciting that it was still being talked about months afterwards. Sally Scott was neck and neck with the Serpentine runner on leg one, passing the baton to Pat Mead just a stride down. Pat tore into the lead but our rivals crept back ahead on leg three as Natalie Stracey gave everything to keep close. The Serps. had a good sprinter in Jane Searle on their final stage and she must have fancied her chances against a Jacinta that was still blowing from the last two races. Both entered the straight together but with everyone from all the clubs leaping with excitement it was the Belgrave girl that got to the line first. Who could ever have imagined that veteran athletics could conjure up such thrills. Captain Snowy was delighted and Herne Hill's Byron Gray is still talking about it being the best race of the year - World Cup and all! Report · page 60

Reebok Southern Veterans' Road Relay, Aldershot, 24 Sep

The Belgrave over sixties stars won back the South of England title they lost last year to Barnet, but it was a pretty close thing with Laurie O'Hara outsprinting Ron Higgs in the final straight winning by a mere six seconds.

Pat Newell had got Bels off to a great start with his fastest ever 60's leg in 23:57 nearly a minute ahead of Cambridge and two and a half minute clear of Bob Belmore of Barnet. But with Laurie Forster and John Wood back to their best, Charlie Walker not going too well and Bert Stroud rather unfit after his holiday, it was Barnet who had pulled into a 26 second lead by the end of the third leg.

After the first lap Laurie had not appeared to have made much impression on the leader Ron Higgs, and even as the runners came into view at the top of the far hill there seemed too much for him to do. But as they raced down the hill on the far side of the playing fields the gap became visibly smaller and as Charlie screamed his instructions to Laurie to "get a move on", Laurie shifted gear at the final bend and the rest, as they say, was history. M60 1, Belgrave 1:35:56 (P.Newell 23:57, C.Walker 25:36, A.Stroud 24:48, L.O'Hara 21:35); 2, Barnet & Dist. 1:36:02; 3, Cambridge H 1:41:26. Fastest: L.O'Hara (Belgrave) 21:35; R.Higgs (Barnet & Dist) 22:08; D.Wood (Barnet & Dist) 23:40.

Report · page 60

Surrey County Veterans CC Champs, Richmond Park, 8 Oct

It was back to the usual lovely Autumn sunshine for this year's popular county championships after the cold day last year. New winners in all categories came from a somewhat reduced field of 157 runners.

An equestrian event on part of the usual course forced the organisers to stage this year's race on the lower Petersham course. This meant climbing that terrible hill on each of the three laps. Another change was to allow the ladies race time to finish before starting the men's and this provided a far better atmosphere for the ladies.

Jacinta Moore ran very well for 5th place, with Nina Mills in 15th and completing the team, Hilary Saw in 21st. Belgrave finished just outside the medals in 4th place.

In the men's race it was again our over sixties who won the medals. Pat Newall, fit at the moment but hating the hill, won the bronze, but the team of Pat, Charlie Walker and Bert Slough again took the team gold with a mere ten points. We even had a fourth scorer in Derek Manning.

Best of the rest was a very characteristically determined run from John Mather in 7th place and an epic struggle between Tony Stone and John Wasbrough in the M50's. John went faster up the hills and Tony overtook when they went down. In the end it was Tony's long legs that prevailed in the sprint to the line.

Belgrave numbers were down on previous years and most hoped the race would return to the old course next year. Notice · page 60

Vets' T&F League up and running

Richard Turner, organiser of the Southern Counties Veterans' T&F League was forced to stand down at the end of the '94 season due to pressure of work and it seemed for a while that the league might fold up. We are still not sure if there is going to be a final in 1995, or even if the other areas are going to continue with their divisions - but the Mid-London Division is up and running again. It is highly likely that each of the clubs we met last year will take part and the likely dates are as follows:

Events are likely to be as in 1994 and once again all clubs are to provide a field judging team, track judge and timekeeper, with the host club taking on all the extra duties.

Mann of the Mountains

If you examine the road running results shown in the pages of this magazine you'll spot the name Mann turning up with startling frequency. Shown below, however, are a whole series of results chalked up by the Mann family - Royal Chef Lionel with sons Robert and James - presumably while accompanying Ma'am on her hols. north of the border this summer. They certainly seem to pick out the tough ones! Two runners went missing on the downhill section of the Cairn William race. At Nairn they placed 8th as a Belgrave team, racing alongside Scottish clubs Lochaber, Arbroath and Inverness (18th were Blackheath), and many times came up against the quaintly named outfit - Cosmic Hillbashers.
